| --- | --- | --- |
| [product_tests.cpp](../../tests/product_tests.cpp) | `product_tests`; CTest `product` | Parser/validation, duplicate/malformed rows, EAN-8 warnings, lookup, stock policies, descriptions, actual fourteen-product fixture, writer/loader round trip. |
| [OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp](../../tests/OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp) | `order_tests`; CTest `order` | Cart arithmetic and invalid quantities, fractional backend weights, IDs, text escaping, order/detail save/load, status updates and malformed records. Plain checks remain active in Release. |
| [integration_test.cpp](../../tests/integration_test.cpp) | `integration_test`; CTest `integration` | Loads actual catalogue, types a known barcode, adapts the product, creates/reads order details and deducts stock in memory. | [checkout_test.cpp](../../tests/checkout_test.cpp) | `checkout_test`; CTest `checkout` | Synthetic product lookup, preview, insufficient cash, successful cash/change, duplicate payment rejection, saved bill and paid-order reload. It does not call Qt `AppState::addOrder`. |
| [billing_tests.cpp](../../tests/billing_tests.cpp) | `billing_tests`; CTest `billing` | Nineteen cases covering arithmetic/rounding/invalid lines, payment strategies, codec errors/round trip, bill IDs and repository append/load/duplicate rejection. |
| [camera_demo.cpp](../../tests/camera_demo.cpp) | Optional `CameraDemo`, not CTest | Standalone Qt camera demonstration using `Frontend/src/ui/CameraWidget.cpp`. Requires an enabled camera build and hardware for capture checks. |

| [Root decoder.cpp](../../decoder.cpp) | Historical/unselected | Root decoder copy; not the decoder selected by active targets. |

These tables cover every checked-in `.cpp` outside generated/dependency `build`
folders. Header files declare interfaces, structs and some inline operations;
they are not additional application entry points.

## Explain one sale from input to disk

1. **Find a product.** A manual code or detected barcode reaches a page. The page
   calls `AppState::findByCode`, which uses the catalogue's barcode/ID/acronym
   search. Barcode decoding and catalogue lookup are separate responsibilities.
2. **Build the visible draft.** Record Order displays a product preview and adds
   integer quantities to its UI cart. Description opens catalogue information.
   Manual items are represented as receipt lines without catalogue stock.
3. **Validate checkout.** The payment dialog collects mode/tender/merchant.
   `AppState::addOrder` rebuilds an `OrderManager` cart from current snapshots,
   checks quantities/combined stock, compares totals and validates payment.
4. **Create the order.** `OrderManager` assigns `YYMM + sequence + DD`, writes
   `OrderDetail.txt` followed by `Order.txt`, and clears its backend cart.
5. **Save inventory.** AppState captures the original product-file bytes, deducts
   catalogue quantities in memory and saves `Product.txt`. Manual items do not
   deduct catalogue stock.
6. **Create the bill.** `BillingService` reloads details and resolves products
   through its injected callback, calculates totals, validates payment again,
   allocates `YYMMDD + sequence`, updates the order to paid and saves `Bill.txt`.
7. **Refresh the screen.** AppState refreshes caches/history; Record Order shows
   the receipt and clears its visible cart after success. Failures retain the
   visible draft for correction; file rollback is attempted as appropriate.

Concrete catalogue example: barcode `40001029` is milk, price 340, tax 0,
discount 10 per unit. Two units total PKR 660; tender 700 gives PKR 40 change.
A successful full checkout should reduce milk stock from 42 to 40 in a fresh
fixture copy and create matching order, detail and bill records. This is an
expected result for a manual case; today's runtime report must establish whether
the GUI path actually achieved it.

## Understand the four persistent files

| File | Writer/owner | What is retained |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Product.txt` | ProductFileWriter / ProductManager | Catalogue identity, name, unit, per-unit price/tax/discount, integer stock and barcode image path |
| `Order.txt` | OrderManager | Order identity/date/time/customer, state, paid amount, payment mode and merchant |
| `OrderDetail.txt` | OrderManager | Order ID, product ID, barcode and quantity |
| `Bill.txt` | BillRecordCodec / BillRepository | One row per item, repeated bill header/totals, saved price/discount/quantity and payment status |

Text records end in `#`. Order/bill text uses escaping for spaces, underscores,
percent signs and terminators. Product names use a different multiword parsing
rule. Bill rows omit per-line tax, cash tender/change and full historical product
names; reloaded display can therefore combine saved totals and current metadata.
See [record layouts](../increment-2/records.md) for exact field order.

Temporary-file replacement and rollback are best-effort recovery, not a
multi-file database transaction. No shared lock/journal coordinates all writers.
Creating an order alone does not complete payment or inventory deduction.

## Build and run the registered backend checks

Run from the repository root with CMake, Ninja and a C++17 compiler available.
Use a new build directory if the existing one belongs to a different compiler.

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build/evaluation-backend -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DBACKEND_ONLY=ON -DCAMERA=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=ON
cmake --build build/evaluation-backend --parallel 4
ctest --test-dir build/evaluation-backend --output-on-failure
ctest --test-dir build/evaluation-backend -N
```

`CAMERA=OFF` is necessary for a dependency-free backend build because camera
discovery precedes the `BACKEND_ONLY` return. `-N` lists the five registered
suites without executing them. Product/integration tests expect the shipped
fourteen-record catalogue; use the CTest-defined working directories.

To run one suite with detailed output:

```powershell
ctest --test-dir build/evaluation-backend -R '^checkout$' -V
```

For a Windows Qt build, use the compiler matching the installed Qt kit. The
following paths describe this workstation and are not universal installation paths:

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build/evaluation-gui -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCAMERA=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=OFF -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH=C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64 -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin/g++.exe
cmake --build build/evaluation-gui --parallel 4
```

For camera work, configure a separate compatible build with `CAMERA=ON` and the
OpenCV installation path as needed. A camera-disabled successful build verifies
neither OpenCV linkage nor capture/decoding.

## Preserve real data during manual evaluation

`AppState::locateDataDir` tries `BARCODEHUB_DATA` first, then executable-relative
and working/source directories. The chosen folder must contain `Product.txt`;
an invalid override falls through to other folders. Copy the complete `data`
directory to a disposable rehearsal location and set `BARCODEHUB_DATA` to that
copy before launching. Verify all four target paths before testing checkout.
The catalogue's `data/barcodes/...` image paths are resolved from the parent of
the selected data folder, so preserve that directory structure in the copy.

The [seed-data script](../../cmake/seed-data.cmake) copies only missing starter
files when the app is built. Rebuilding is not an inventory reset. The
[Windows build helper](../../scripts/build-windows.ps1) also prepares dependencies,
tests and deployment; those packaging actions are separate from the core sale flow.

## What a complete evaluation must still distinguish

| Evidence | What it establishes | What it does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Backend build + five passing CTest suites | Current backend compiles and those assertions pass | GUI wiring, camera, printer, rollback faults, concurrent checkout |
| Qt app build | Current selected frontend sources compile/link | Successful launch or every user action |
| Launch and page navigation | App loads the chosen data and renders reachable pages | Correct settlement/persistence |
| Disposable checkout + file comparison + restart | That tested sale persisted expected records and reloads | Crash safety or all payment boundaries |
| Real camera frame capture | Selected camera supplies usable frames | Successful barcode decoding |
| Successful known-barcode detection | That fixture/lighting/orientation decoded | General accuracy for all labels |

Today's runtime outcomes belong in the companion evaluation report, including
exact build configuration, passing/failed cases and untested hardware paths.
Keep source-derived expectations separate from observed results.

FILE: /docs/evaluation/manual-testing.md

# Increment 2 manual evaluation guide — 19 November 2026

This is a repeatable test procedure for BarcodeHub's current Qt interface.
Every case below starts **Pending**. Expected results come from source inspection;
they become actual results only after a tester performs and records the action.
Build success and backend tests do not establish that a GUI or camera case passed.
The live-test operator should record observations separately using the case IDs.

## Prepare a reproducible session

1. Record the tested commit, executable path, build configuration, camera option,
   Windows version, and test time. Record any source changes during testing.
2. Use a complete disposable copy of `data`, including its barcode PNGs. Preserve
   an untouched copy for resetting between cases. Point `BARCODEHUB_DATA` at the
   copied directory **before launching**; it must contain `Product.txt`.
3. Verify the runtime catalogue starts with milk stock 42 and bread stock 34.
   Clear transaction files only in a dedicated fixture prepared for that purpose;
   otherwise record initial order counts/sales and evaluate the change in totals.
4. Start one application instance. Stop camera capture before switching between
   Record Order and Barcode Scanner camera panels.
5. Replace the sample customer and phone with `Evaluation Cash` and `03001234567`.
   Default sample identity text is not evidence of login/authentication.
6. Capture before/after evidence for checkout. A real checkout writes inventory,
   order headers, order details, and bills in the selected copied directory.

If `BARCODEHUB_DATA` lacks Product.txt, directory selection can fall back to a
different candidate. Confirm the chosen runtime directory rather than assuming
the repository's files and runtime files are identical.

## Reference fixture and arithmetic

| Product | Barcode | Price | Tax/unit | Discount/unit | Initial stock |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Olpers Full Cream Milk 1L | `40001029` | 340 | 0 | 10 | 42 |
| National Whole Wheat Bread | `40002149` | 260 | 18 | 0 | 34 |
| Farm Fresh Eggs Dozen | `40007359` | 390 | 0 | 0 | 4 |
| Coca Cola 1.5L | `40011721` | 230 | 18 | 0 | 0 |

The main checkout fixture is **two milk plus one bread**:

- Subtotal: `2 × 340 + 260 = PKR 940`.
- Discount: `2 × 10 = PKR 20`; tax: `1 × 18 = PKR 18`.
- Payable: `940 - 20 + 18 = PKR 938`.
- Cash tendered PKR 1,000 gives change PKR 62.
- After successful checkout milk stock becomes **40** and bread stock **33**.

These exact values require an untouched catalogue fixture. Record actual starting
stock when rerunning a checkout; each additional transaction deducts stock again.

## Startup, navigation, and products

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NAV-01 | Launch the current executable | Record Order opens; empty cart; seven sidebar destinations are present | Pending |
| NAV-02 | Visit Dashboard, Record Order, Orders, Products, Barcode Scanner, Bills, Settings | Each destination opens without crash; title/subtitle follow the page | Pending |
| NAV-03 | Add a draft item, visit Dashboard, use New Order | Existing cart is retained; Dashboard New Order is navigation | Pending |
| PRD-01 | Products: search `milk`, `OLPMILK`, then `40001029` | Relevant milk record appears; exact values match the runtime catalogue | Pending |
| PRD-02 | Open milk Details | Description shows product fields, stock status, and barcode image when path resolves | Pending |
| PRD-03 | Products: search `zzzz-no-match` | Empty result set, no stale matched row | Pending |
| PRD-04 | Products: find eggs and cola | Eggs is Low at default threshold 10; cola is Out and its Add is disabled | Pending |
| PRD-05 | With auto-add off, Products: click milk Add | Record Order opens and previews milk; explicit Add to order is still needed | Pending |
| PRD-06 | Dashboard lookup: enter `40001029`, then an actual saved order ID | Product code opens Products search; an unmatched product code is routed to Orders search | Pending |

Catalogue text search covers name/acronym/barcode. The exact code lookup additionally
supports numeric product ID (`1`) and acronym (`OLPMILK`). Record a mismatch if a
screen's placeholder promises broader text matching than the actual search provides.

## Manual entry, cart, custom items, and stock

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CART-01 | Empty draft; type `40001029`, press Enter | Milk preview appears; cart stays empty until Add to order | Pending |
| CART-02 | Click Description, close it, click Add to order | Correct product description; one milk in cart, payable PKR 330 | Pending |
| CART-03 | Press milk plus once; enter bread barcode, Enter, Add to order | Two milk/one bread; three item units; totals 940/20/18/938 | Pending |
| CART-04 | Press bread minus at quantity one | Bread row disappears and totals return to milk-only values | Pending |
| CART-05 | Add bread again, then use its trash button | Only bread is removed; milk quantity remains two | Pending |
| CART-06 | Use Browse/F4 on Record Order; select bread | Product preview changes; choosing a product does not add it automatically | Pending |
| CART-07 | Empty input, Enter; then unknown code `99999999`, Enter | Informative toast; no new cart row; unknown lookup clears preview | Pending |
| CART-08 | Start a disposable draft; add eggs and increase to four, then press plus | Four is allowed; fifth is rejected; total stays PKR 1,560 | Pending |
| CART-09 | Try to add zero-stock cola through manual lookup | Product can be described; Add to order is disabled | Pending |
| CART-10 | Custom item: description `Evaluation service`, price PKR 25.50 | Manual line quantity one; total PKR 25.50, zero default tax/discount | Pending |
| CART-11 | Cancel custom description; repeat with blank description | Neither attempt adds a row | Pending |
| CART-12 | Enter a custom description, then cancel price | No custom row is added | Pending |
| CART-13 | Increase custom quantity to two | Total PKR 51.00; no catalogue inventory entry changes | Pending |
| CART-14 | Clear/Ctrl+N on a disposable draft | Cart and preview clear; customer/contact are retained | Pending |

Use Clear between custom/stock cases and the main payment fixture. Current UI
quantity controls use whole integers even for products labeled `weight`.
Duplicate catalogue acronyms can merge different products into one cart row;
the shipped fixture has distinct acronyms. Do not alter it during acceptance runs.

## Cash checkout, cancellation, and persisted evidence

Recreate the main two-milk/one-bread fixture with starting stock 42/34. Use the
customer `Evaluation Cash`. Record the displayed order ID before payment.

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PAY-01 | Empty draft: press F10 | Empty-order toast; no payment dialog or saved order | Pending |
| PAY-02 | Main fixture: Checkout/F10 | Instant Payment dialog shows PKR 938; Cash initially selected | Pending |
| PAY-03 | Set tender to PKR 900 | Short by PKR 38; Confirm Payment disabled | Pending |
| PAY-04 | Set tender to PKR 938 | Confirmation enabled; change PKR 0 | Pending |
| PAY-05 | Cancel payment | Draft remains; stocks/order counts/files unchanged | Pending |
| PAY-06 | Reopen; tender PKR 1,000; Confirm Payment | Receipt opens with real order and bill IDs; payable 938, tender 1,000, change 62 | Pending |
| PAY-07 | Close receipt | Cart clears; customer/contact stay; next order ID appears | Pending |
| PAY-08 | Products: inspect milk and bread | Stocks are now milk 40, bread 33 | Pending |
| PAY-09 | Inspect copied transaction files read-only | Matching order is paid; detail rows contain milk quantity two/bread one; bill stores totals 940/18/20/938 | Pending |

Keep the generated order/bill IDs for history and restart cases. Receipt is a text
viewer: no print/PDF action is implemented. Card/wallet are local payment records,
not bank authorization or settlement.

## Card, wallet, and custom-item checkout

Run each case on its own draft. Keep the main cash receipt separate so later
dashboard comparisons account for these additional sales.

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MODE-01 | One milk, Checkout, Card; leave merchant blank, then spaces | Confirmation disabled | Pending |
| MODE-02 | Card: merchant `HBL`; Confirm Payment | Paid order/bill for PKR 330; Card recorded; milk stock decreases by one; no tender/change shown | Pending |
| MODE-03 | One bread, Checkout, Wallet; merchant blank | Confirmation disabled | Pending |
| MODE-04 | Wallet: merchant `JazzCash`; Confirm Payment | Paid order/bill for PKR 278; Wallet recorded; bread stock decreases by one | Pending |
| MODE-05 | Checkout a PKR 25.50 custom item with exact cash | Paid custom line; bill retained; catalogue inventory unchanged | Pending |
| MODE-06 | Open each resulting order's details/receipt | Payment mode and amount match the corresponding transaction | Pending |

Merchant text is validated again by the backend; enabled UI confirmation alone
does not establish that every possible merchant value will be accepted.

## Orders, bills, CSV, dashboard, and restart

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIST-01 | Orders: All; search saved cash order ID, then customer/phone | Correct row found; details match three units and PKR 938 | Pending |
| HIST-02 | Orders: clear search; choose Paid, In Progress, On Hold | Rows follow selected status; empty tabs are acceptable for a paid-only fixture | Pending |
| HIST-03 | Orders: View details then Receipt | Detail SUBTOTAL is unit-price × qty; receipt separately shows tax/discount and final total | Pending |
| HIST-04 | Bills: search saved bill ID, order ID, customer | Paid bill appears with actual bill number and payable PKR 938 | Pending |
| HIST-05 | Filter Orders to one row; Export CSV to a disposable output file | CSV contains all loaded orders, regardless of active search/status filter | Pending |
| HIST-06 | Cancel Export CSV save dialog | No export file created and no success toast | Pending |
| DASH-01 | After main cash transaction only, compare Dashboard to baseline | Today's paid revenue rises 938; today's order count rises one | Pending |
| DASH-02 | Inspect average/recent orders/hourly chart | Average uses paid orders; recent list includes newest order; chart includes it only at hours 08–20 | Pending |
| LIFE-01 | Leave one unpaid draft line; close; choose No | Application stays open with draft intact | Pending |
| LIFE-02 | Close; choose Yes; restart same data copy | Paid history/bills and stock persist; unpaid draft is lost | Pending |
| LIFE-03 | Reopen cash receipt after restart | Payable remains 938; cash tender/change are not restored | Pending |
| LIFE-04 | Reopen custom receipt after restart | Saved price/quantity/totals persist; custom name falls back to saved acronym | Pending |

CSV embedded quotes are not escaped; this can produce malformed exported text for
names containing quotes. An empty history screen can indicate a malformed record
load rather than empty files: inspect warning output and all transaction files.

The beep checkbox currently has no sound implementation; record that limitation
without treating a silent scan as a failed decoder. Active shortcut routing is in
`MainWindow::handleFKey()`, not the unused `NavigationModel::items()` list.

## Camera acceptance and fallback

A `CAMERA=OFF` executable cannot establish camera readiness. Record a build with
camera support and actual hardware trial separately. The two pages own separate
camera widgets. Stop one before starting the other to avoid device contention.

| ID | Steps | Expected result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CAM-01 | Record Order: Start Cam or F9 | Live frames appear, or a clear error explains device/backend failure | Pending |
| CAM-02 | Hold a clear milk EAN-8 image flat, steady, with even light | Decoded `40001029` matches milk preview; no wrong product is accepted | Pending |
| CAM-03 | Auto-add off: detect milk | Preview changes without cart mutation | Pending |
| CAM-04 | Auto-add on: detect milk once, then move barcode away | Quantity rises by one after successful lookup/stock validation | Pending |
| CAM-05 | Keep same barcode visible over three seconds | Duplicate suppression can expire and emit it again; record actual repeated additions | Pending |
| CAM-06 | Stop Record Order camera; open Scanner and Start Cam/Start Scan | Scanner frames appear; detected code enters Recent Scans | Pending |
| CAM-07 | Scanner: Show description; Add last scan to order | Description matches; shared route previews/adds according to auto-add preference | Pending |
| CAM-08 | Stop camera; type milk barcode in Scanner and Lookup | Product and recent-scan display update without hardware capture | Pending |

If device opening fails, record the exact message and hardware/build context;
mark decoding cases Blocked rather than Passed. Manual lookup is the presentation
fallback. It tests product/cart flow but does not prove camera decoding works.

## Record actual results

Use `Pass`, `Fail`, `Blocked`, or `Not run`, keeping evidence separate from expected
behavior. If execution has started, replace Pending with the actual result only.

| Case ID | Actual result | Evidence/file or screenshot | Build/data directory | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fill after execution | Not run | None yet | Record both paths | Record observed value/message |

Known boundaries to mention during evaluation: whole-number UI quantities;
in-memory draft/customer retention; local card/wallet recording; no printer/PDF,
refund or hold/resume controls; no live file reload; non-atomic multi-file checkout;
08:00–20:00 chart window; archived orders counted as pending; and header/identity
fixture text that can differ from runtime behavior. A running GUI proves startup,
not every behavior listed above.

[pages and settings](../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp),
[navigation](../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp),

# BarcodeHub evaluation: build, run, test and explain

Prepared on 19 November 2026 for the local Increment 2 evaluation. Application
source baseline: `514aa7e`. Test data and temporary test programs are under the
ignored `build` folder; application business logic was not changed.

## Open the camera-enabled application

Double-click [Run-Camera-Evaluation.cmd](../../Run-Camera-Evaluation.cmd).
It opens the freshly built application with its separate evaluation data:

```text
D:\Github-Repositories\Barcode-POS-System\build\evaluation-camera\BarcodeHub.exe
D:\Github-Repositories\Barcode-POS-System\build\evaluation-camera\data
```

The original `Run-BarcodeHub.cmd` configures new builds with `CAMERA=OFF`.
That explains the screenshot's "Camera support is disabled" message. The new
evaluation executable was explicitly configured with `CAMERA=ON`, compiled,
linked, and supplied with Qt and OpenCV runtime DLLs. Opening the old executable
can reproduce the old message even though the new build supports a camera.

Evaluation checkout updates only the selected evaluation data copy. The original
repository `data` directory is not the checkout destination. Repeated evaluation
sales reduce the evaluation stock further; record starting stock before a test.

## What to read

- [Manual test checklist: 68 cases](manual-testing.md): exact UI steps, sample
  values, negative cases, restart and settings checks.
- [C++ file-by-file map](backend-file-map.md): every `.cpp`, active versus
  historical files, module ownership, and the complete execution flow.
- [Scanner execution results](scanner-results.md): 89 image checks, real camera
  open/capture/reopen results, and the live recognition attempt.
- [Qt interface test results](ui-test-results.md): observed automated widget
  results, test limitations, and the isolated fixture used.

## Build and test again on this machine

Run PowerShell from the repository root. This uses the installed Qt compiler
and an existing OpenCV cache built with that same compiler; it does not rebuild
OpenCV or modify the sibling repository.

```powershell
$env:PATH = 'C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin;C:/Qt/Tools/Ninja;C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64/bin;' + $env:PATH
cmake -S . -B build/evaluation-camera -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin/g++.exe -DCMAKE_MAKE_PROGRAM=C:/Qt/Tools/Ninja/ninja.exe -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH=C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64 -DOpenCV_DIR=D:/Github-Repositories/Bar-Code-System-CPP/build/deps/opencv/x64/mingw/lib -DCAMERA=ON -DBUILD_TESTING=ON
cmake --build build/evaluation-camera --parallel 4
ctest --test-dir build/evaluation-camera --output-on-failure
& 'C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64/bin/windeployqt.exe' --release --no-translations --compiler-runtime build/evaluation-camera/BarcodeHub.exe
& ./Run-Camera-Evaluation.cmd
```

The MinGW Debug application links the installed release Qt libraries, so the
successful deployment command uses `windeployqt --release`. An initial
`--debug` deployment attempt could not find a debug platform plugin; the
release-runtime deployment succeeded. The latest CTest run passed all five
suites in 1.28 seconds. CameraDemo also compiled in this camera-enabled build.

If this OpenCV cache is absent on a different PC, the existing
[Windows build script](../../scripts/build-windows.ps1) can download/build the
dependency and assemble a portable application. That full script was not run
for this evaluation because a matching cache already existed.

## A ten-minute evaluation sequence

1. **Explain the architecture.** The Qt GUI accepts input; AppState coordinates
   catalogue, order and billing services; four local text files store data.
2. **Show Products.** Search `40001029`, open Details, explain price, unit,
   tax, discount, available stock and the barcode image.
3. **Show the camera.** Use only one camera panel at a time. Click Start camera,
   hold the label upright with vertical bars and visible white side margins,
   and watch for the full eight-digit code. The supported format is EAN-8.
   If recognition fails, stop capture and use typed lookup for the sale.
4. **Build the sale explicitly.** On Record Order enter milk `40001029`, press
   Enter, click Add to order, increase milk quantity to two; enter bread
   `40002149`, press Enter, click Add to order once.
5. **Explain the arithmetic.** Subtotal 940, discount 20, tax 18, payable
   **PKR 938**. These values assume the supplied catalogue prices.
6. **Show validation.** In payment, PKR 900 is insufficient. PKR 1,000 pays
   the bill and produces **PKR 62 change**. Use evaluation data for confirmation.
7. **Show evidence.** Read the receipt; locate the order in Orders and its bill
   in Bills. For the untouched starting fixture, milk stock changes 42→40 and
   bread 34→33. Two bill item rows share a single bill ID and order ID.
8. **Show alternatives.** Card and Wallet require merchant names. These are
   locally recorded payment modes; there is no bank authorization connection.
9. **Show reports and preferences.** Orders supports CSV export; Dashboard shows
   today's counters. Apply the low-stock threshold or dark mode in Settings.
10. **Explain persistence and limits.** Restart restores saved sales/stock, while
    the cart is lost. Receipts are text views, without printer/PDF integration.

## Explain the whole system in one minute

"BarcodeHub is a C++17 desktop point-of-sale system built with Qt Widgets.
Products are loaded from Product.txt. A cashier types or scans an EAN-8 barcode
to find a product, checks its description and stock, and builds a cart. The
order module validates quantities and creates an eight-digit order ID. The
billing module calculates per-unit tax and discount, validates the selected
cash/card/wallet payment, creates a nine-digit bill ID, and saves the sale.
The GUI coordinator saves stock changes and attempts rollback if saving fails.
Orders, Bills and Dashboard display the saved results. Storage is local text
files; payment processing is simulated locally rather than connected to a bank."

## Functionality checklist for the evaluator

Use the detailed manual guide for individual acceptance steps. A feature can
have passing backend/widget evidence while still needing human interaction or
physical-camera verification.

- [ ] Startup and all seven pages.
- [ ] Product search, description, barcode image and load issues.
- [ ] Camera start, video, live barcode recognition, stop and restart.
- [ ] Manual barcode/ID/acronym lookup and unknown-code handling.
- [ ] Explicit Add, auto-add preference and repeat-scan quantity behavior.
- [ ] Cart plus/minus, removal, Clear and retained customer fields.
- [ ] Zero-stock and combined-quantity rejection.
- [ ] Custom item entry, price, quantity and cancellation.
- [ ] Subtotal, tax, discount and payable calculations.
- [ ] Cash shortfall, exact payment, change and payment cancellation.
- [ ] Card and Wallet merchant validation and local recording.
- [ ] Receipt IDs and amounts; completed-sale draft reset.
- [ ] Order/detail/bill persistence and product stock persistence.
- [ ] Orders search/filter/details and CSV export.
- [ ] Bills search and historical receipts.
- [ ] Dashboard daily counters, hourly chart and low-stock indicators.
- [ ] Settings Apply, dark mode persistence and session-only preferences.
- [ ] Context-dependent keyboard shortcuts.
- [ ] Restart with the same data copy.

## Scope to state accurately

- The active scanner reads EAN-8, even though one legacy API is named decodeSix.
- The active POS target does not provide the historical generator/image-upload UI.
- Backend order code allows fractional weight quantities; the GUI uses integers.
- Card/Wallet validate merchant text and save records without external settlement.
- Settings' beep flag has no implemented scan audio action.
- Hold/resume drafts, refunds, login/roles and inventory purchasing are not active
  cashier workflows in this implementation.
- Temporary files and rollback attempts are not a crash-safe transaction across
  the four files, and there is no multi-process checkout locking.
- The live camera probe captured frames but decoded no label during its timed
  attempt. Do not present fixture-image success as a successful physical scan.

# Scanner execution results - 19 November 2026

The current scanner source passed **89 isolated checks**, and camera index 0
successfully opened through DirectShow and returned 640 by 480 BGR frames.
A user-coordinated live scan then read 832 frames over 30.27 seconds but decoded
no barcode. Camera release/reopen also passed. No camera image was saved.

## Scope and toolchain

The probe compiled the checked-in [CameraScanner implementation](../../Backend/src/scanner/CameraScanner.cpp)
and [decoder](../../Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp) directly. The execution
checkout was `514aa7e`; the probe did not alter application code or CMake.
Its C++ harness and output remain local ignored artifacts under
`build/evaluation-probes/`, separate from the five registered CTest suites.

The compiler was `C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin/g++.exe` (GNU 13.1.0).
OpenCV 4.13.0 was reused from an existing matching-compiler installation at
`D:/Github-Repositories/Bar-Code-System-CPP/build/deps/opencv`.
No dependency download, OpenCV rebuild, or sibling-repository mutation was needed.

## Observed image and decoder results

Each image case required `frameRead()` to return success and exactly match the
full eight-digit barcode in the shipped PNG's filename. All 14 catalogue PNGs
were tested in every image group below.

| Group | Passed / attempted |
| --- | ---: |
| Original BGR images | 14 / 14 |
| Grayscale conversions | 14 / 14 |
| BGRA conversions | 14 / 14 |
| 90-degree clockwise rotations | 14 / 14 |
| 180-degree rotations | 14 / 14 |
| Horizontal mirrors | 14 / 14 |
| Blank image, empty frame, stale-output clearing | 3 / 3 |
| Synthetic valid EAN-8 and wrong-checksum EAN-8 | 2 / 2 |
| **Total** | **89 / 89** |

The synthetic pair used exact 67-module EAN-8 layouts with white margins:
`40001029` decoded, while `40001020` was rejected with the bad-checksum flag.
The invalid-checksum image changes only the last digit's valid bar pattern.
Blank/empty frames returned false and cleared prior output; the empty frame
reported `Camera stopped sending frames`. `decodeSix("bad", output)` also
cleared a prior output string rather than returning stale digits.

## Actual camera result

The separate `--camera` invocation called the application's `openCamera()`
and then `readFrame()` once. It printed:

```text
CAMERA_OPEN PASS
CAMERA_FRAME PASS dimensions=640x480 channels=3
CAMERA_PROBE_EXIT=0
```

The scanner destructor released the camera when the process exited. No frame
was encoded, displayed, saved, or sent elsewhere by this probe. The earlier
absence of an inventory entry did not predict the actual device-open result.

## Live recognition attempt and camera lifecycle

After the user confirmed the webcam was connected and a barcode was ready,
the extended probe opened index 0 and repeatedly called the application's
`readFrame()` and `frameRead()` until a code was found or 30 seconds elapsed.
The expected example was `40001029`; any actually decoded code would have
been printed. The observed output was:

```text
LIVE_OPEN PASS scan_seconds=30
LIVE_SCAN NO_CODE code= expected=40001029 frames=832 seconds=30.2715 fps=27.4846 read_ok=1
LIVE_REOPEN PASS frame=PASS
```

The live probe exited 4 because the recognition window ended without a code.
Frame capture itself succeeded. Its camera object was destroyed before a new
scanner reopened the same device and successfully read another frame. The
measured rate is approximately 27.48 frames per second for this invocation,
including capture/decoding and startup overhead; it is not a device guarantee.
This attempt does not establish why recognition failed. Fixture PNG success
does not establish equivalent lighting, focus, scale or alignment at the lens.

## Commands used

Run from the repository root. The local probe source was generated for this
evaluation and is not an application source or committed test target.

```powershell
& 'C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin/g++.exe' -std=c++17 -O0 -g build/evaluation-probes/scanner_probe.cpp Backend/src/scanner/CameraScanner.cpp Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp -IBackend/include/scanner -I'D:/Github-Repositories/Bar-Code-System-CPP/build/deps/opencv/include' -L'D:/Github-Repositories/Bar-Code-System-CPP/build/deps/opencv/x64/mingw/lib' -lopencv_videoio4130 -lopencv_imgcodecs4130 -lopencv_imgproc4130 -lopencv_core4130 -lole32 -loleaut32 -lstrmiids -o build/evaluation-probes/scanner_probe.exe
$env:PATH = 'C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin;D:/Github-Repositories/Bar-Code-System-CPP/build/deps/opencv/x64/mingw/bin;' + $env:PATH
& ./build/evaluation-probes/scanner_probe.exe data/barcodes
& ./build/evaluation-probes/scanner_probe.exe --camera
& ./build/evaluation-probes/scanner_probe.exe --camera-scan
```

Image checks and the single-frame camera check exited zero. The live
recognition attempt exited 4 as described above. Local logs are
`build/evaluation-probes/scanner-images.log`, `scanner-camera.log`, and
`scanner-live.log`.

## Remaining presentation checks

These results do not test the Qt camera feed, repeated-scan suppression,
automatic cart addition, extended sessions beyond the 30-second attempt,
disconnect/reconnect recovery, lighting variation, or arbitrary skew. Recognition
of the presented barcode through the lens was attempted but did not succeed.
The successful rotated fixture cases do not remove the source's conditional
rotation limitation. Use the [camera worksheet](../increment-2/presentation/scanner-rehearsal.md)
to record any later GUI/hardware demonstration separately.

# Build options and active source map

Use this reference when explaining which parts of Increment 2 a build or test
actually exercises. It describes the checked-in CMake configuration; target
availability does not mean that the target compiled successfully.

## Option combinations

| Configuration | Available components | External requirements |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BACKEND_ONLY=ON`, `CAMERA=OFF`, `BUILD_TESTING=ON` | Backend libraries and five test executables | C++17 compiler, CMake |
| `BACKEND_ONLY=ON`, `CAMERA=ON` | Backend plus scanner library; tests if enabled | OpenCV 4, even without Qt |
| `BACKEND_ONLY=OFF`, `CAMERA=OFF` | Backend, Qt UI, BarcodeHub; tests if enabled | Qt Widgets and compatible compiler |
| `BACKEND_ONLY=OFF`, `CAMERA=ON` | Above plus scanner and optional CameraDemo | Qt Widgets and OpenCV 4 |
| `CAMERA=AUTO` | Scanner only when OpenCV is discovered | Camera can be absent without configuration failure |
| `BUILD_TESTING=OFF` | Omits the five registered test executables | Does not repair or bypass GUI compilation |

`CAMERA` defaults to `ON`; `BACKEND_ONLY` defaults to `OFF`. OpenCV discovery
precedes the backend-only return. Specify `CAMERA=OFF` explicitly when testing
the backend without OpenCV. `CAMERA=OFF` still allows manual product lookup.

## Targets and responsibilities

| Target | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `product_core` | Catalogue model, parsing, validation and stock |
| `order_core` | Cart, order records, details and order states |
| `common_core` | Shared record-text and date helpers |
| `billing_core` | Totals, payment validation, codec and bill repository |
| `integration_core` | Product-to-order snapshot adapter |
| `backend_core` | Interface target joining backend dependencies |
| `scanner_core` | OpenCV capture, localization and EAN-8 decoder |
| `barcode_ui` / `BarcodeHub` | Qt pages/widgets and application entry point |
| `CameraDemo` | Standalone camera widget, when camera support is enabled |

CTest names are `product`, `order`, `integration`, `checkout`, and `billing`.
Their executables are `product_tests`, `order_tests`, `integration_test`,
`checkout_test`, and `billing_tests`. None is a live GUI/camera acceptance test.

## Distinguish active and older sources

| Feature boundary | Selected by current CMake | Older or separate material |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Application entry | `Frontend/src/main.cpp` | `Frontend/src/ui/main.cpp` |
| Main camera view | `CameraFeedWidget` in `Widgets.cpp` | `POSWindow.cpp` and `Frontend/legacy-camera/` |
| Scanner implementation | `Backend/src/scanner/CameraScanner.cpp`, `decoder.cpp` | Root-level scanner/decoder copies |
| Optional camera window | `tests/camera_demo.cpp`, UI `CameraWidget.cpp` | Not registered in CTest |
| Generator regression | No generator target in current configuration | `tests/regression.cpp` references absent historical APIs |

The CMake scanner source and some includes use `cameraScanner` casing while
tracked files use `CameraScanner`; case-sensitive environments need review.
The backend passed five tests. The initial `1b16a70` GUI build failed on stale
`RecordOrderPage` declarations; a separate correction at `883425c` allowed the
Qt build with `CAMERA=OFF` to pass. Camera-enabled builds and GUI interaction
remain unverified. See [verification](../verification.md).

Sources: [CMakeLists.txt](../../../CMakeLists.txt),
[presets](../../../CMakePresets.json), [current main](../../../Frontend/src/main.cpp),
and [older regression](../../../tests/regression.cpp).

## Match a command to a defensible claim

| Command/target result | Supported claim | Remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Configure with BACKEND_ONLY=ON and CAMERA=OFF | Required backend toolchain was found | No compilation or assertions yet |
| Build the backend test executables | Backend source compiles and links | Tests must still execute |
| CTest reports five passes | Those registered backend assertions passed | No Qt checkout or camera coverage |
| Build BarcodeHub with CAMERA=OFF | Qt application compiles and links | No live UI or OpenCV capture verified |
| Build BarcodeHub_deploy | Deployment tool completed for that build | Copied package must still be launched |
| Launch CameraDemo | That separate widget can start | Main POS widget has different capture handling |

Always record the source revision, option values and build directory with the
result. Reusing an old executable is not evidence that a new source tree built.

# Data lifetime and reload behavior

Use this map to explain what survives navigation, checkout, and application exit.
Initial review used `1b16a70`; the GUI build with `CAMERA=OFF` passes at `883425c`.
GUI/camera interactions are unexecuted. Storage uses text files and one QSettings preference.

## Ownership and lifetime

| Value | Owner/location | When it changes or reloads | After restart |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Active cart | RecordOrderPage memory | Explicit add/remove/quantity actions; clear; successful checkout | Lost |
| Customer/contact fields | RecordOrderPage widgets | Cashier edits; initial sample defaults | Initial defaults return |
| Product catalogue/stock cache | ProductManager and AppState memory | Startup load, checkout deduction, rollback reload | Loaded from Product.txt |
| Inventory | Selected Product.txt | Successful save during checkout | Retained |
| Orders and details | Selected Order.txt/OrderDetail.txt | Order creation/update and recovery operations | Retained |
| Bills | Selected Bill.txt | Successful billing append | Retained |
| History display cache | AppState memory | Startup and successful checkout reconstruction | Rebuilt from files |
| Cash tendered | Current OrderRecord | Current payment dialog | Not restored from records |
| Low-stock threshold | Settings memory | Apply button | Defaults to 10 |
| Auto-add preference | Settings memory | Apply button | Defaults to false |
| Beep preference | Settings memory | Apply button; no audio action implemented | Defaults to true |
| Dark mode | BarcodeHub/SmartPOS QSettings | Apply when changed | Retained |
| Reorder markers | AppState in-memory set | Dormant reorder methods | Lost; no active reorder UI |

## Navigation is not a reset

Changing pages retains the current draft and customer/contact fields. Dashboard
New Order navigates to Record Order without clearing an existing cart.
Ctrl+N or Clear empties the cart, but it also retains customer/contact fields.
Successful checkout clears the cart after receipt dismissal and retains those
fields too. Check the customer's name before the next transaction.

## External file edits are not live updates

There is no active file watcher or visible Reload button. Normal catalogue
loading happens at startup; stock rollback also reloads it. Successful checkout
refreshes the catalogue cache from its existing in-memory manager, rather than
reading arbitrary external catalogue edits anew. History is reconstructed at
startup and after checkout, so navigation alone does not reread record files.

For rehearsal data changes, close the application, prepare a complete copied
data set, then restart with the intended directory. Avoid editing files while
a session is using its cache: a later save can replace external edits with the
session's values. Concurrent application instances have no file-lock protocol.

## Startup and daily values

Data-directory selection looks for Product.txt; it does not validate all four
files before choosing a candidate. Product-load issues and history-load errors
follow different reporting paths. See [history diagnostics](history-diagnostics.md).
Daily sales/order counters are calculated during history reconstruction, with
no midnight refresh timer. Restart or checkout can change the displayed day totals.

Sources: [AppState](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp),
[draft lifecycle](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp),
[navigation](../../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp),
[settings controls](../../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp),
and [theme settings](../../../Frontend/src/ui/Theme.cpp).

## A valid data override can still have broken pictures

BARCODEHUB_DATA selects a directory by the presence of Product.txt. Relative
barcode image paths are resolved against that directory's parent, not against
the directory itself. This matches the shipped path data/barcodes/40001029.png
when the chosen directory is named data inside the copied project/package.

For example, choosing D:/Demo/data resolves that image to
D:/Demo/data/barcodes/40001029.png. Choosing D:/Demo/catalogue with the same
unmodified path still resolves under D:/Demo/data/barcodes. Keep the expected
layout when preparing a demo copy; successful product parsing alone does not
establish that the image exists. See m_root and ProductInfo::from in
[AppState](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).

# Portable application rehearsal worksheet

This is an unexecuted packaging and launch checklist. Packaging was inspected
from source and was not run for this documentation change. The initial
`1b16a70` GUI compilation failed; a separate correction at `883425c` allowed
the Qt build with `CAMERA=OFF` to pass. Camera-enabled builds, portable launch
and live GUI interaction remain unverified. Record the source revision of
any existing executable before using it as presentation evidence.

## Identify the compiler and dependencies

The Windows helper searches for a Qt 6 MinGW kit and reads its configured GCC
major/minor version to find a matching compiler. Pass explicit `QtRoot` and
`MinGWRoot` values if automatic selection does not identify the intended kit.
Do not mix binaries built for unrelated compiler ABIs.

The script requests OpenCV 4.13.0 and builds `core`, `imgproc`, `imgcodecs`,
and `videoio` when its dependency cache is missing. Windows capture uses
DirectShow; the script disables FFmpeg and Media Foundation. This package
configuration does not establish support for every camera or video format.

Packaging is a build operation: it can download dependencies, modify cached
OpenCV source for MinGW compatibility, run tests, copy DLLs, and create files.
It is not a read-only inspection command.

## Check the proposed package contents

The helper assembles `build/portable/BarcodeHub`. After a successful build,
inspect the resulting folder before transferring it to the presentation PC.

| Item | Purpose / acceptance question | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BarcodeHub.exe` | Does this correspond to the intended source revision? | Not checked |
| Qt runtime DLLs | Were dependencies copied by the matching `windeployqt`? | Not checked |
| Qt platform plugin | Can the app start with the deployed Windows plugin? | Not checked |
| Compiler runtime DLLs | Are the matching MinGW runtimes available? | Not checked |
| OpenCV runtime DLLs | Are camera-enabled dependencies beside the executable? | Not checked |
| `data/Product.txt` | Does the selected data folder load the intended catalogue? | Not checked |
| Barcode PNGs | Do product detail images resolve from the packaged data? | Not checked |
| Other transaction data files | Are rehearsal records separate from live records? | Not checked |
| `licenses/` | Are application, OpenCV and available dependency notices included? | Not checked |

The seed script copies only missing starter files; rebuilding an existing
package preserves its transaction files and stock. It does not reset the demo.
For repeatable rehearsal, prepare a separate copy of the complete data folder.

## Rehearse the transferred folder

1. Record the source revision and the package's build date before transfer.
2. Launch from the copied folder without relying on the developer's Qt shell.
3. Confirm the actual data selection; `BARCODEHUB_DATA` can override defaults.
4. Check Products and one barcode image before creating a transaction.
5. Use the [scanner worksheet](scanner-rehearsal.md) if camera capture is needed.
6. Follow the [transaction walkthrough](../demo.md) using disposable records.
7. Close and reopen to verify the saved order, bill and stock in that data copy.

A successful backend CTest run does not verify transferred DLLs, device access,
Qt rendering, or GUI persistence. Record those outcomes only after observing them.

Sources: [Windows helper](../../../scripts/build-windows.ps1),
[deployment targets](../../../CMakeLists.txt),
[seed policy](../../../cmake/seed-data.cmake), and
[data discovery](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).

## Diagnose a launch failure before changing records

| Launch symptom | First evidence to collect |
| --- | --- |
| Missing DLL message | Exact DLL name and copied package contents |
| Qt platform-plugin error | Platform plugin directory and matching Qt deployment |
| App starts but products differ | BARCODEHUB_DATA and executable-relative data candidates |
| Product values load but images fail | Stored image token and parent-relative resolved path |
| Camera control reports disabled | CAMERA build option used for this executable |
| Camera control cannot open a device | Device availability and whether another page/process owns it |

A camera-disabled build can be a useful manual-entry rehearsal artifact, but
copying OpenCV DLLs beside it does not enable camera code that was compiled out.
Keep the source revision and build options beside the package when presenting
which capabilities it actually contains.

FILE: /docs/increment-2/presentation/ean8-worked-example.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Explain a scanned EAN-8 code

Use the milk catalogue entry `40001029` to explain how a picture becomes a
product lookup. This is a worked source example, not a recorded camera test.

## Check the eighth digit

EAN-8 uses seven data digits followed by one check digit. The implementation
weights the first seven positions alternately by three and one.

| Position | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Digit | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 2 |
| Weight | 3 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 3 |
| Product | 12 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 3 | 0 | 6 |

The weighted sum is 21. The check digit is `(10 - (21 % 10)) % 10 = 9`.
Therefore `40001029` passes the checksum. A checksum-valid code can still be
absent from the catalogue; product lookup is a separate operation.

## Explain the bars as 67 modules

A module is the narrow unit used to express bar and space widths. Here `1`
means a black module and `0` a white module. Guards separate the digit groups.

```text
start  left digits: 4       0       0       0       center
101                 0100011 0001101 0001101 0001101 01010

right digits: 1       0       2       9       end
              1100110 1110010 1101100 1110100 101
```

The layout contains `3 + (4 * 7) + 5 + (4 * 7) + 3 = 67` modules.
Right-side digit patterns are the bitwise complements of the left-side
patterns. The decoder measures 43 alternating stripe runs, estimates a
module width, rounds each run to one through four modules, and validates
the guards and digit patterns before checking the checksum.

## Connect decoding to the POS

The historical API name `decodeSix()` returns the full **eight-digit** code.
The output `40001029` matches the barcode field in `Product.txt`; the GUI can
then preview the product and explicitly add it to an order. The method name
does not mean that the current catalogue uses six-digit barcode keys.

The main POS does not expose a barcode-generation screen. The unregistered
older regression file references generator APIs absent from the current tree.
Use the existing catalogue PNG when explaining this example.

Sources: [decoder](../../../Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp),
[decoder contract](../../../Backend/include/scanner/decoder.h),
[EAN-8 helper](../../../Backend/include/product/Ean8.h),
[catalogue](../../../data/Product.txt), and
[legacy regression source](../../../tests/regression.cpp).

## Compare the decoder with catalogue validation

Change only the final digit of the example to produce 40001028. Its first
seven digits still require check digit 9, so the camera decoder rejects that
candidate. The catalogue's BarcodeRule instead reports a checksum warning and
can retain a row with an eight-digit numeric code that has the wrong checksum.

Consequently a product reachable through typed catalogue lookup need not be
readable from a barcode image with that same invalid check digit. This is a
difference between the implemented validators, not a claim that malformed
labels were tested on hardware. See
[BarcodeRule](../../../Backend/src/product/ProductValidation.cpp) and
[decoder checksum validation](../../../Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Diagnosing empty or unexpected history

Initial review used `1b16a70`; final source check is `883425c`.
The GUI build with `CAMERA=OFF` passes; GUI and camera interactions remain unexecuted.
Use copied data when reproducing malformed records or reload differences.

## Empty Orders or Bills does not establish that files are empty

`AppState::refreshOrders()` clears the cached order list and today's counters,
then loads `Order.txt`, `OrderDetail.txt`, and `Bill.txt`. If any load fails, it
returns before rebuilding history and writes a `qWarning` message:

```text
Could not load persisted orders/bills: <loader error>
```

There is no connected GUI history-error banner in this path. A malformed bill
can therefore hide otherwise valid orders in the current cached view. Consult
the application's captured warning output and validate all three record files.
Do not delete transaction files to make the screen appear populated.

## Check the selected directory first

`BARCODEHUB_DATA` is a preferred candidate only when it contains `Product.txt`.
Otherwise the application searches executable-relative, working-directory,
and source-directory candidates. A valid but unintended runtime data copy can
explain why the history differs from the repository's `data` folder.
See [build and run](../build.md) for candidate ordering.

## Distinguish Orders from Bills filtering

Orders applies the selected status tab and its text search. Clear the search
and choose All when checking whether a loaded order exists. Bills requires both
`Paid` status and a nonempty ID from an actual saved bill. A paid order without
a corresponding loaded bill will not appear there.

## Keep detail order aligned with saved bill lines

History finds a bill by order ID, then uses the matching order-detail row index
to select `bill.lines[detailIndex]`. It does not join those lines by product ID
or verify the saved acronym against each detail. Manually reordering details
can attach the wrong historical unit price/discount to a displayed product.
Keep the original files together when comparing a restored transaction.

Aggregate subtotal, discount, tax and payable are overwritten from the saved
bill when present. A correct aggregate total alone does not prove every
displayed product line was reconstructed correctly.

## Explain differences between views

Order Details shows each product's `unit price × quantity` as SUBTOTAL.
Its bottom total also reflects tax and discounts, so those numbers need not sum
directly without the adjustments. Receipt text exposes the aggregate adjustments.
Historical catalogue names come from the current catalogue; custom descriptions
fall back to saved acronyms after restart. Cash tender/change are session data.

Sources: [history loader](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp),
[Orders/Bills filtering](../../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp),
[detail and receipt views](../../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp),
and [record format reference](../records.md).

## Worked example of positional reconstruction

Suppose one saved order has detail rows [milk, bread], and its bill lines store
[milk at 340, bread at 260]. The normal row ordering pairs those amounts with
the intended products. Reversing only the detail rows to [bread, milk] makes
the loader use the first saved bill line for bread and the second for milk.

The aggregate payable can still come directly from the bill and look correct.
Use this as an explanation of a source limitation; do not reorder real records
to demonstrate it. An actual failure reproduction would need a disposable data
copy and a running GUI, neither of which was exercised for this case.
See detailIndex in [history reconstruction](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).

# Identifier and precision examples

Inspection began at `1b16a70`; final verification used revision `883425c`.
Backend CTest passed 5/5 and the Qt GUI built with `CAMERA=OFF`.
Interactive GUI/camera operation remains untested. These source-inspected examples
were not executed as additional tests.

## Order versus bill identifiers

| Identifier | Layout | Example for 19 November 2026 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| First order | `YYMM` + daily sequence `01` + `DD` | `26110119` |
| Second order | `YYMM` + daily sequence `02` + `DD` | `26110219` |
| Last permitted order sequence | `YYMM` + `99` + `DD` | `26119919` |
| First bill | `YYMMDD` + daily sequence `001` | `261119001` |
| Second bill | `YYMMDD` + daily sequence `002` | `261119002` |
| Last permitted bill sequence | `YYMMDD` + `999` | `261119999` |

The next sequence is one above the highest matching persisted ID, not the number
of records plus one. Gaps are not filled. Orders permit 99 sequences per day;
bills permit 999. Reaching either maximum produces an error.
`nextOrderId()` previews an ID without reserving it. Deleting the highest order
can allow its sequence to be reused because allocation only inspects current data.

[Order allocation](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp) and
[bill allocation](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp) use local
dates. The format checks are not complete calendar validation, and there is no
lock preventing concurrent callers from proposing the same next ID.

## Monetary arithmetic

The [calculator](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillCalculator.cpp) accumulates
subtotal, tax and discount separately, rounds each to two decimals, then rounds
`subtotal + tax - discount`. Tax/discount inputs are PKR per unit.

| Example | Expected result/explanation |
| --- | --- |
| Two units: price 250, tax 12.50, discount 10 | Subtotal 500, tax 25, discount 20, payable 505 |
| Two lines each priced 0.004, quantity 1, no tax/discount | Aggregate subtotal 0.008 rounds to 0.01; no per-line rounding first |
| Ordinary stored money | Two decimal places in bill/order records |
| Fractional quantities | Three decimal places, trailing zeros removed |

Floating-point `double` and `std::round` are used. They do not provide integer
minor-unit accounting; binary representation affects exact decimal boundaries.
See [RecordText helpers](../../../Backend/src/common/RecordText.cpp).

## Source-inspected precision gaps

**Tiny weighted quantity:** `0.0001` is finite and positive, so a backend weight
cart can accept it. Order writing formats it to three decimals as `0`; reloading
then rejects the nonpositive detail quantity. No minimum precision is enforced.
Compare quantity validation and `formatQuantity` in
[OrderManager.cpp](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp).

**Large finite values:** a price of `1e308` and quantity 2 pass the calculator's
individual finite checks, but multiplication exceeds finite `double` range.
There is no post-calculation finite check, so the input checks alone do not
guarantee finite totals. This is an unexecuted boundary finding, not a test result.

**Historical reconstruction:** bill rows store aggregate tax but omit each
line's tax and the original full product name. Reloaded display data can combine
saved amounts with current catalogue metadata; see [record layouts](../records.md).

## Rollover example and date-validation boundary

If the final saved order on 19 November is 26110819, the next is
26110919. On 20 November, with no matching IDs for that day, the first is
26110120; the matching bill prefix changes from 261119 to 261120.
The order format places its day at the end; the bill format places it before
the sequence. Neither is a monotonic global transaction counter.

Order::isValid checks date/time separators and digit positions rather than
real calendar or clock ranges. Correct shape alone does not reject month 99
or hour 99. Generated timestamps use the local clock; manually supplied records
need stronger semantic checks than those format checks provide.
Evidence: [Order validation](../../../Backend/src/order/Order.cpp).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Worked payment acceptance cases

Inspection began at `1b16a70`; final verification used revision `883425c`.
Backend CTest passed 5/5 and the Qt GUI built with `CAMERA=OFF`.
Interactive GUI/camera operation remains untested. Cases below describe source
behavior, not newly executed payment transactions.

## Cash example from the actual catalogue

[Milk `40001029`](../../../data/Product.txt) costs PKR 340 with zero tax and
PKR 10 discount per unit. Two units total `680 + 0 - 20 = PKR 660`.
This differs from the synthetic PKR 505 example in the backend checkout test.

| Tender/request | Expected local result for payable 660 | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cash 660 | Accept; change 0 | Exact amount |
| Cash 700 | Accept; change 40 | Tender exceeds payable |
| Cash 659 | Reject | Insufficient tender |
| Cash -1 | Reject | Negative tender |
| Cash infinity/NaN | Reject | Tender must be finite |
| Cash 0 | Reject for this bill | Zero is below payable, although not intrinsically invalid tender |
| Card, merchant `HBL` | Accept local payment rule | Nonblank name without `#` |
| Wallet, merchant `JazzCash` | Accept local payment rule | Same merchant validation |
| Card/wallet, blank or spaces | Reject | Merchant name required |
| Card/wallet, merchant `Bank#1` | Reject | Delimiter prohibited |

[PaymentMethod.cpp](../../../Backend/src/billing/PaymentMethod.cpp) supplies
these rules. Card/wallet ignore cash tender and do not authorize or settle with
an external provider. A locally accepted rule can still be followed by a file error.

## Zero-total boundary: rule success versus checkout failure

Consider one item with price 100, tax 0, discount 100, quantity 1:

| Stage | Expected source behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Bill calculation | Accepts payable 0; only a negative total is rejected |
| Cash rule with tender 0 | Accepts and returns change 0 |
| Card/wallet with valid merchant | Accepts merchant text |
| Mark order paid with paymentAmount 0 | Rejects: paid orders require a positive amount |
| Full checkout | Fails before saving a finalized bill |

This boundary is source-inspected, not a newly run zero-total test.
Evidence: [calculator](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillCalculator.cpp),
[payment rules](../../../Backend/src/billing/PaymentMethod.cpp),
[paid-order validation](../../../Backend/src/order/Order.cpp), and
[service sequence](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillingService.cpp).

## State and persistence boundaries

`BillingService::checkout` rejects paid and archived orders before payment;
an on-hold order is eligible. A bill save failure triggers an attempt to restore
the previous order state. [Qt checkout](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp)
additionally orchestrates stock restoration and removal of the new unpaid order.

[checkout_test.cpp](../../../tests/checkout_test.cpp) executes a synthetic
two-unit bill totaling 505: tender 500 fails, tender 510 returns 5, and repeat
checkout fails. [billing_tests.cpp](../../../tests/billing_tests.cpp) covers
cash, merchant validation and default strategies. Neither proves bank settlement.

## Explain the three cash amounts separately

For the milk-and-bread rehearsal, payable is PKR 938. If the customer hands over
PKR 1,000, change is PKR 62. The paid order records paymentAmount as 938, not
1,000. The bill repeats the payable total; neither file stores tender or change.

| Value | Current-session receipt | Restored transaction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Amount payable | 938 | Saved as 938 |
| Cash received | 1,000 | Not persisted |
| Change | 62 | Cannot be recovered uniquely from saved totals |

This explains why a historical receipt can show the sale total but cannot
prove how much cash was handed over. See the assignment to
paidOrder.paymentAmount in [BillingService](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillingService.cpp).

FILE: /docs/increment-2/presentation/persistence-failure-cases.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Persistence failure walkthrough

Inspection began at `1b16a70`; final verification used revision `883425c`.
Backend CTest passed 5/5 and the Qt GUI built with `CAMERA=OFF`.
Interactive GUI/camera operation remains untested; failures were not injected.
Use these source-inspected cases to explain recovery, not to claim tested crash
or disk-failure guarantees.

## Which component owns each stage?

| Stage/failure | Attempted behavior | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Validate cart/tender before writes | Return error before creating an order | [AppState::addOrder](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp) |
| Write new order details fails | Return failure; order header is not written | [OrderManager::createOrder](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp) |
| Details saved, order header fails | Attempt to restore old detail rows | Same order manager |
| Original product file cannot be opened for backup | Attempt to discard new in-progress order | AppState |
| Deducted stock cannot be saved | Restore original product bytes; discard new order | AppState |
| Bill history cannot load / ID allocation fails | Return billing failure before marking paid | [BillingService](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillingService.cpp) |
| Marking order paid fails | Do not append bill | BillingService |
| Bill append fails after order marked paid | Attempt to restore previous order state | BillingService |
| Any billing failure reaches AppState | Restore product bytes; attempt to discard new order | AppState |

Creating an order and settling it are separate backend operations. Stock rollback
belongs to AppState, not BillingService. A backend-only caller must supply its
own inventory orchestration if it needs equivalent behavior.

## Replacement and rollback limits

- Order/detail and bill writers stage `<file>.tmp`, then rename it into place.
- When rename-over-existing fails, they fall back to `copy_file(...overwrite_existing)`.
- Product saving uses a similar temporary-file replacement strategy.
- Qt stock restoration uses `QSaveFile` to replace the backed-up product bytes.
- Some rollback return values are ignored; recovery itself may fail.
- Checked Qt restoration/discard failures add `rollback needs manual attention`
  to the reported checkout error.

Evidence: [order writers](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp),
[bill writer](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp),
[product writer](../../../Backend/src/product/ProductStorage.cpp) and
[Qt restoration](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).

## Questions to answer during a presentation

**Can a power loss leave stock/order/bill inconsistent?** Yes. Four files are
updated separately. There is no transaction journal, commit marker or process
lock coordinating all writes; successful temporary-file writes do not make the
whole checkout crash-safe.

**Can two running copies safely sell the final item?** The code has no shared
lock or reservation. Both can read old stock; concurrent checkout is unverified.

**What should be checked after a failed demo checkout?** In a disposable data
copy, compare all four files with the pre-demo backup. Check order/detail IDs,
paid status, corresponding bill rows and catalogue stock together. Preserve the
error and backup before any manual recovery; no automated repair tool is supplied.

**What has actually been tested?** Existing backend tests cover successful file
round trips and normal rejection paths. They do not simulate power loss, full
disks or rollback failures. See [verification](../verification.md).

## Preserve the relevant evidence when a read fails

| Reported stage | Files to compare with the same backup set | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Product lookup or stock save | Product.txt and referenced barcode path | Product metadata and image resolution are separate |
| Order lookup | Order.txt | Header identity, state and payment are stored here |
| Empty/missing order products | OrderDetail.txt together with Order.txt | Detail rows require the correct order identity |
| Bill parsing or history display | Bill.txt together with both order files | History combines all three record sources |
| Inconsistent inventory after failure | All four files | A checkout spans multiple independent replacements |

A single file from an older backup can disagree with the other three even if
every row parses. Match order IDs, bill order references and item quantities
before drawing conclusions from screen totals. No repair action was executed
as part of this review.

# Follow-up review results - 19 November 2026

Initial baseline: branch `ali`, commit `1b16a70`; final source recheck at
`883425c`. This is a follow-up to the
[original verification](../verification.md), with an additional source review
of backend, GUI, scanner, active targets, and legacy paths.

## Observed checks

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `git fetch origin` | Completed successfully. |
| `git pull --ff-only origin ali` | Already up to date. |
| Branch compared with fetched `origin/main` | 33 commits ahead, 0 behind at review baseline. |
| Open pull requests through public GitHub API | Empty list returned; no open PR to inspect or take. |
| Backend Debug incremental build | Passed; Ninja reported no work required. |
| Backend CTest | All five suites passed, 0 failures; total 2.20 seconds. |
| Initial Qt GUI rebuild, camera disabled, at `1b16a70` | Failed again at missing `RecordOrderPage` members. |
| Final Qt GUI rebuild, camera disabled, at `883425c` | Passed; `BarcodeHub.exe` linked after the separately committed header repair. |

The backend rerun reused the configured build directory from the earlier fresh
build. It is not a second clean-build claim. No camera runtime, GUI checkout,
portable package, or physical receipt printer was tested.

## Reproduction commands

Run from the repository root using the matching toolchain. See the
[build guide](../build.md) for configure commands if these directories do not exist.

```powershell
cmake --build build/increment2-audit-backend --parallel 4
ctest --test-dir build/increment2-audit-backend --output-on-failure
$env:PATH = 'C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin;C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64/bin;' + $env:PATH
cmake --build build/increment2-audit-gui --parallel 4
```

The initial GUI compiler report at
[RecordOrderPage.cpp:61](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp#L61):

```text
error: 'm_feed' was not declared in this scope
```

Further diagnostics included `m_customer`, `m_contact`, `m_manual`, `m_sessionId`,
`m_checkout`, and total labels. Compare the
[header](../../../Frontend/include/ui/RecordOrderPage.h) with its implementation.
During this review another process committed `46f1a4b` (a project text dump),
then `883425c` (the header repair). Rebuilding after those commits completed
successfully, including linking `BarcodeHub.exe`. This documentation work did
not author either commit or change application source. Camera-enabled compilation
and interactive behavior remain unverified.

## Additional source findings

- History loading clears cached history before reading all three record files;
  a malformed file can make history appear empty. See [diagnostics](history-diagnostics.md).
- Saved bill lines are paired with order details by position during history
  reconstruction. Preserve item order across the files.
- External file edits have no automatic refresh UI. See [data lifecycle](data-lifecycle.md).
- Quantity serialization has three decimal places, while backend weight
  validation accepts smaller positives. Billing checks finite inputs without a
  final finite-result check. See [precision boundaries](identifiers-precision.md).

These findings are source-inspected boundaries, not newly executed failure tests.
The [screen](screen-rehearsal.md), [payment](payment-cases.md), and
[scanner](scanner-rehearsal.md) cases are an explicit plan for further checking.

# Camera rehearsal and acceptance worksheet

These cases are **planned and unexecuted**. No camera hardware or GUI behavior
was verified during this documentation work. The initial `1b16a70` GUI build
failed on mismatched `RecordOrderPage` members; a separate header correction
at `883425c` allowed the Qt build with `CAMERA=OFF` to pass. A camera-enabled
build and live capture remain unverified. See [verification](../verification.md)
before using this worksheet for a live presentation.

## Prepare one capture session

1. Use a verified camera-enabled build and disposable application data.
2. Confirm that the selected data folder contains the sample catalogue.
3. Stop camera capture on the other POS page before starting this feed.
4. Use the catalogue image for `40001029` with visible white margins.
5. Begin with horizontal, well-lit bars and keep the image steady.
6. Leave automatic scan addition off to separate preview from explicit Add.

The main feed opens camera index 0. It requests 640 by 480 MJPG frames at
30 FPS; these settings are requests, not proof of device capabilities.
The Qt capture timer runs every 40 ms and performs decoding on that callback.

## Record observed outcomes

| Case | Expected from source | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start with working device | Live frames replace the stopped-camera view | Not run |
| Present `40001029` | Eight-digit code is emitted and milk lookup succeeds | Not run |
| Keep same code visible for under three seconds | Repeated code emission is suppressed | Not run |
| Keep same code visible beyond three seconds | A later detection may emit again | Not run |
| Replace with `40002149` immediately | Different code can emit without waiting | Not run |
| Present a blank white image | No valid barcode detection | Not run |
| Present bars with a wrong check digit | Decoder rejects that candidate | Not run |
| Present a vertical code | Conditional rotation may decode; success is not guaranteed | Not run |
| Stop the main feed | Timer stops, scanner is destroyed, camera is released | Not run |
| Capture fails after start | Feed stops and emits a scan-error message | Not run |
| Build with `CAMERA=OFF` | Start reports disabled camera support | Not run |

The decoder is EAN-8-specific. QR, EAN-13, arbitrary skew, damaged labels,
blurred frames, and every camera model are outside established coverage.
The 90-degree retry occurs only when contour localization finds no suitable
box, so vertical-code handling depends on the surrounding image too.

## Use the manual fallback

Stop capture, type `40001029` on Record Order, press Enter, and explicitly
click Add to order. This exercises catalogue lookup and the cart without
establishing that camera decoding worked. If the presentation machine cannot
run the GUI, use the backend evidence in the [demo guide](../demo.md).

The optional CameraDemo uses a different widget with no repeat suppression;
its behavior does not establish the main POS feed's acceptance results.

Sources: [main camera feed](../../../Frontend/src/ui/Widgets.cpp),
[capture/localization](../../../Backend/src/scanner/CameraScanner.cpp),
[decoder](../../../Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp),
[order page](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp), and
[CameraDemo widget](../../../Frontend/src/ui/CameraWidget.cpp).

## Separate successful decoding from a successful product match

A decoded EAN-8 value can be absent from the current catalogue. For such a
label the camera may emit a valid code while Record Order reports no product.
Record both the emitted digits and the lookup result when diagnosing a demo.

| Observation | What it establishes | What still needs checking |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Frame visible but no digits | Camera capture started | Label orientation, sharpness and decoder acceptance |
| Digits emitted but unknown product | Decoder found a valid candidate | Selected catalogue and exact barcode key |
| Correct preview but empty cart | Lookup succeeded | Explicit Add or scanner auto-add preference |
| Quantity increases twice | Addition occurred twice | Repeat detection interval and auto-add setting |

These are source-based diagnostic cases; none was run with camera hardware.

The stock, order, detail, and bill files must belong to the same rehearsal set.

## Hardware checks

Stop capture on one camera page before starting the other. Start one camera,
present a clear fixture barcode, then confirm the product preview and emitted code.
Record the device and lighting if this passes; it is separate from manual lookup.
With auto-add enabled, holding a barcode in view can add it again after roughly
three seconds. Move it away after the intended detection and inspect quantity.

## Record useful evidence

For each passed row, record the source commit, executable build directory, data
directory, action, and observed result. For failed rows, keep the exact compiler
or UI message and the data copy used. Do not label pending rows as passed merely
because the five backend tests pass.

## Check the copied data after the worked sale

These are pending acceptance checks for the shipped fixture, not observed GUI

two milk and one bread, and compare the records before and after:

| Evidence | Expected change for one successful checkout |
| --- | --- |
| Product.txt | Milk 42 to 40; bread 34 to 33 |
| Order.txt | One new paid header with paymentAmount 938 |
| OrderDetail.txt | Two matching rows: milk quantity 2, bread quantity 1 |
| Bill.txt | Two new rows sharing one bill ID and order ID; payable 938 |
| Second checkout attempt on that saved order | Backend rejects already-paid order |

Record the baseline ID set instead of assuming the data files were empty.
Closing the receipt should clear the draft; it should not remove saved records.
