# Keyboard cases for presentation rehearsal

Initial review used `1b16a70`; the GUI build with `CAMERA=OFF` passes at `883425c`.
All cases below are source-derived expectations; GUI and camera trials are unexecuted.
Test with no modal dialog open, as focus can affect delivery. Use a disposable data copy.

## Context changes the action

| Starting context | Key | Expected action | Rehearsal detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Any main page | F1 | Open Dashboard | Existing cart is retained |
| Any main page | F2 | Open Record Order and focus input | Does not create a new draft |
| Any main page | F3 | Open Orders | Existing search/filter can remain |
| Record Order | F4 | Open Browse products chooser | Choosing an item previews it; no auto-add through this manual path |
| Any other page | F4 | Open Products | Sidebar label describes this general destination |
| Any main page | F5 | Open Barcode Scanner | Does not itself start capture |
| Any main page | F6 | Open Bills | Only paid orders with real bill IDs are listed |
| Record Order | F9 | Start its camera if stopped | Despite method name simulateScan, this starts capture |
| Barcode Scanner | F9 | Navigate to Record Order and start that camera | Does not invoke the Scanner screen's Start Scan button |
| Other main page | F9 | Navigate to Record Order and start its camera | Stop any other capture first |
| Record Order, empty cart | F10 | Call checkout and show empty-order toast | It does not open Settings here |
| Record Order, populated cart | F10 | Open payment dialog | Cancelling preserves the draft |
| Any other page | F10 | Open Settings | No checkout occurs through this key in that context |
| Any main page | Ctrl+K | Open Dashboard and focus lookup | Lookup resolves a product code first, otherwise searches Orders |
| Any main page | Ctrl+N | Open Record Order and clear draft | Customer/contact fields are retained |

## Suggested three-minute keyboard rehearsal

1. Put one milk unit in a draft using manual entry and Add to order.
2. Press F1, then F2; verify the draft survived and input has focus.
3. Press F4; select bread; verify preview changes before explicitly adding.
4. Press F10; cancel; verify neither draft contents nor history changed.
5. Press F3, then F10; verify Settings opens rather than payment.
6. Return with F2; check the customer fields; use Ctrl+N on the disposable draft.
7. Test F9 separately after confirming only one camera page is active.

## Which source defines these shortcuts?

`MainWindow` creates QShortcut objects and routes them through `handleFKey()`.
Its local navigation table builds the sidebar. This is the active authority for
the context-dependent F4/F10/F9 behavior described above.

`NavigationModel::items()` also lists page shortcuts, but no consumer is present
in the active frontend sources. Updating that list alone would not change the
MainWindow routing. The RecordOrderPage header's F4 Description comment also
differs from `quickLookup()`, which opens Browse products in the implementation.
Use visible Details/Description buttons when demonstrating product descriptions.

Sources: [MainWindow routing](../../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp),
[unused navigation list](../../../Frontend/src/ui/NavigationModel.cpp),
[Record Order handlers](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp),
and [existing settings guide](../settings.md).

## Trace manual entry without confusing focus, preview and add

On Record Order, F2 focuses the barcode field. Typing 40001029 and pressing
Enter calls the manual preview path; it is not an instruction to add a cart
unit. The visible Add to order button then performs the addition. Auto-add is
checked only for the scanner-style path, so switching that preference does not
change the manual Enter sequence.

In a rehearsal, note the cart quantity after each of these three actions.
This separates a keyboard-routing issue from a lookup issue or stock rejection.
See focusInput, showCode, and the manual field's returnPressed connection
in [RecordOrderPage](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp).

# Validation cases across layers

Inspection began at `1b16a70`; final verification used revision `883425c`.
Backend suites passed 5/5 and the Qt GUI built with `CAMERA=OFF`.
Interactive GUI/camera operation remains untested. Rows below are source-inspected
examples, not newly executed test results; consult linked assertions for tested cases.

## Catalogue loading versus ordering

| Input/example | Catalogue rule | Order/cart rule | Practical consequence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Barcode `40001029` | Eight digits and valid checksum | Snapshot needs nonempty barcode without whitespace | Known fixture can be ordered |
| Eight digits with wrong checksum | Warning; row retained | No checksum verification in cart | A warning does not prohibit a sale |
| Seven-digit barcode | Row rejected | Backend snapshot alone does not enforce EAN-8 length | Use the catalogue loader before ordering |
| Duplicate product ID or barcode | Later row rejected | Cart merges by product ID | ID is the backend cart identity |
| Duplicate acronym | Not rejected | Backend IDs remain distinct | GUI acronym-based merging is a separate risk |
| Acronym length 9 | Default rule allows up to 10 | No acronym-length check in cart | Increment plan's limit of 8 differs |
| Price `nan` | `strtod` and sign checks can allow it | Nonfinite price rejected | Later validation adds protection |
| Price 100, tax 10, discount 105 | Catalogue rejects discount above price | Snapshot permits discount up to price + tax | Layers have different contracts |
| Empty barcode image path | Rejected | Not represented in snapshot | Cart cannot validate an image path |

Evidence: [product rules](../../../Backend/src/product/ProductValidation.cpp),
[loader](../../../Backend/src/product/ProductStorage.cpp),
[cart validation](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp), and
[GUI cart](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp).

## Quantity examples

| Example | Expected source behavior | Existing test reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Count product, quantity 1.5 | Reject: whole number required | [order smoke test](../../../tests/OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp) |
| Weight product, quantity 1.5 | Backend cart accepts within stock | Same order smoke test |
| Stock 5, add 2 then 4 | Reject combined quantity 6; retain previous line | Same order smoke test |
| Quantity zero/negative | Reject before cart update | Same order smoke test |
| Product stock zero, request 1 | Reject for insufficient stock | [product tests](../../../tests/product_tests.cpp) |
| Weight quantity 0.0001 | Positive input passes backend validation; stored quantity rounds to zero | Source inspection only; precision gap |

Quantity records use three decimals; see [precision examples](identifiers-precision.md).
Successful backend acceptance of a weight fraction does not imply UI support.

## File validation and error scope

| File/load | Example problem | Scope of failure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Product catalogue | One malformed row among valid rows | Reject that row; keep accepted rows and issues |
| `ProductManager::loadProducts` | Mixed valid/rejected rows | Return false but replace memory with accepted rows |
| Order file | Duplicate order ID, bad enum or missing `#` | Fail the load with line-specific error |
| Detail file | Nonpositive quantity or malformed identity | Fail the load |
| Bill file | Bad nine-digit bill ID or nonfinite number | Fail the load |
| Bill file | Negative finite quantity or inconsistent repeated totals | Parser/grouping lacks full business validation |

See [order record validation](../../../Backend/src/order/Order.cpp),
[detail validation](../../../Backend/src/order/OrderDetail.cpp),
[bill parsing](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRecordCodec.cpp) and
[bill grouping](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp).
Do not equate syntactically valid stored text with a valid business transaction.

## Status validation is not a complete workflow state machine

The following are source expectations, not new executed tests. An updated
record must first pass Order::isValid; updateOrder then adds the restriction
that an archived order cannot reopen.

| Starting state | Requested operation | Current backend boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| In-progress | Update to on-hold | Allowed for an otherwise valid record |
| Paid | Update to in-progress | Not prohibited by updateOrder; no refund is generated |
| Archived | Update to in-progress | Rejected |
| Archived | Update while staying archived | Allowed for an otherwise valid record |
| On-hold | Billing checkout | Eligible; normal payment/persistence rules still apply |
| Paid | Billing checkout again | Rejected as already paid |

The GUI does not expose all these mutations. An enum listing four states does
not establish a persisted hold/resume/refund interface.

FILE: /docs/increment-2/presentation/viva-backend.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Backend viva practice

Inspection began at `1b16a70`; final verification used revision `883425c`.
Backend CTest passed 5/5 and the Qt GUI built with `CAMERA=OFF`.
Interactive GUI checkout and hardware camera operation remain untested.

## Questions about the design

**Why separate Product, Order, and Billing?** Product owns catalogue values,
lookup and stock; Order owns cart rules and order records; Billing owns totals,
payment selection and bill records. Each can be exercised without Qt.
See [CMake targets](../../../CMakeLists.txt).

**Where is the Single Responsibility Principle visible?**
[BillCalculator](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillCalculator.cpp) calculates;
[BillRecordCodec](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRecordCodec.cpp) translates
text records; [BillRepository](../../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp)
loads/saves them. Changing display styling does not require these components.

**What does the adapter do?**
[ProductAdapter](../../../Backend/src/integration/ProductAdapter.cpp) copies a
catalogue `Product` into `ProductSnapshot`, including converting its integer ID
to text. Order code therefore does not include Product's implementation.

**How is Billing independent of the catalogue?** Its constructor receives a
`ProductLookup` callback. The GUI supplies catalogue/manual-item lookup and tests
can supply a synthetic product. See
[BillingService](../../../Backend/include/billing/BillingService.h).

**Where is the Open/Closed Principle visible, and what is its limit?**
[IProductRule and IStockPolicy](../../../Backend/include/product/ProductValidation.h)
permit new validation/policy implementations. Payment strategies share
[IPaymentMethod](../../../Backend/include/billing/PaymentMethod.h).
A truly new payment mode still needs changes to the `PaymentMode` enum,
serialization and GUI selection; the registry alone does not remove those changes.

## Questions about actual behavior

**Does creating an order reduce stock?** `OrderManager::createOrder` saves an
in-progress order and clears its cart. Qt's
[AppState::addOrder](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp) separately saves
stock and invokes billing. Backend checkout by itself does not own inventory.

**Are tax and discount percentages?** Both are PKR amounts per unit. Two units
priced at 250 with tax 12.50 and discount 10 total `500 + 25 - 20 = 505`.

**Does a card payment contact a bank?** No external payment gateway is called.
[Card/wallet strategies](../../../Backend/src/billing/PaymentMethod.cpp)
validate merchant text locally; cash validates tender and calculates change.

**Can weighted products use fractions?** Backend order quantities are `double`
and allow fractions for `weight`. Catalogue stock and current UI quantities are
integers, so fractional checkout is not implemented throughout the GUI.

**Are text files a transaction system?** Writers use temporary files and attempt
rollback. Files are updated separately and no journal/process lock coordinates
them. Describe recovery as best effort, not crash-safe atomic checkout.

**What do passing tests prove?** The five registered suites exercise backend
rules and normal file persistence. They do not prove camera operation or GUI
checkout. See [verification](../verification.md) and [coverage](../../../tests/checkout_test.cpp).

## Trace one catalogue purchase aloud

For milk product ID 1, ProductManager finds the catalogue object using barcode
40001029. ProductAdapter::toSnapshot copies ID, price, tax, discount, unit,
and available stock into the order representation. The backend cart validates
the requested and combined quantity before createOrder saves detail/header
records. BillingService::preview resolves the saved product ID again and
calculates a bill; checkout validates payment, marks paid and appends the bill.
In the GUI, AppState additionally coordinates the inventory save and rollback.

The boundary matters: neither a successful lookup nor a successful order save
means a finalized sale exists. Point to the paid order and its associated bill
when explaining completion. Stock persistence is an additional GUI-owned step.

# GUI viva: questions and evidence

Initial source review: `ali` at `1b16a70`, 19 November 2026; rechecked at `883425c`.
The GUI build with `CAMERA=OFF` now passes after the header repair in `883425c`.
These answers describe implementation; interactive GUI and camera checks are unexecuted.

## What does the cashier interface actually do?

It provides product lookup, a cart, quantity controls, customer fields, payment
selection, receipt preview, and views of saved orders and bills. `MainWindow`
connects pages; `AppState` translates between Qt data and the C++ backend.
Start with [MainWindow](../../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp) and
[AppState](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp) when explaining integration.

## Is the displayed cashier a logged-in user?

No authentication flow is present in the active application. The sidebar text
`Alex Rivera`, `Cashier`, and `Terminal 04` is hard-coded display content.
The initial draft also contains `Marcus Vance` and a sample telephone number.
Replace customer fields before a real demonstration transaction.
See [MainWindow](../../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp) and
[RecordOrderPage](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp).

## Why does an order's shift text change after restart?

Checkout sets the display shift to `POS #04 • Shift A`; history reconstruction
sets `POS #01` and the customer tier to `Guest`. Neither shift nor tier is stored
in `Order.txt`. These labels are UI metadata, not audited cashier identities.
The persisted fields are explained in [records](../records.md).

## Does clicking Products Add always increase the quantity?

The button sends the barcode to Record Order through the scan-style entry path.
With auto-add disabled, the product is previewed and the cashier must use
`Add to order`. With auto-add enabled, one unit is added after lookup succeeds.
This differs from typing a barcode, which previews it regardless of auto-add.
See `showCode()` in [RecordOrderPage](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp).

## Are card and wallet payments connected to a bank?

They validate merchant text locally and record the selected payment mode.
The application has no gateway authorization, settlement, or refund operation.
Cash shows tender/change; all three modes still pass backend payment validation.
See [payment dialog](../../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp) and [payments](../payments.md).

## Can the receipt be printed or downloaded as PDF?

The current receipt is formatted text in a modal viewer with a Close button.
There is no print/PDF action in that viewer. Orders offers a separate CSV export;
CSV is a transaction table rather than a receipt rendering.

## What can I claim about testing?

Five backend CTest suites passed in the current inspection. They do not execute
the Qt widgets or `AppState::addOrder()`. The GUI build at `883425c` passes with
`CAMERA=OFF`; interactive checkout and a camera trial remain separate acceptance steps.
Use [verification](../verification.md) for the earlier recorded test coverage
and [screen rehearsal](screen-rehearsal.md) for pending GUI checks.

## How is a custom item different from a catalogue product?

The custom-item dialog supplies a description and positive unit price. The
draft gives it a generated CUSTOM-prefixed acronym, count unit and zero tax/
discount. It has no catalogue product ID and does not reduce catalogue stock.
AppState builds a temporary CUSTOM-prefixed snapshot for this checkout so
billing can still resolve the line and persist its amount.

The full custom description is session metadata. After restart, a historical
custom line can fall back to the saved acronym because the bill schema does
not store a complete product name. A manual line therefore demonstrates billing
flexibility, rather than catalogue creation or inventory replenishment.
Evidence: [custom dialog](../../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp),
[draft conversion](../../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp), and
[AppState snapshots](../../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).

# Architecture and active entry points

The application separates the C++17 backend from Qt Widgets presentation.
[CMakeLists.txt](../../CMakeLists.txt) is the authority for which files are built.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Main[Frontend/src/main.cpp] --> UI[barcode_ui: MainWindow and pages]
    UI --> State[AppState: Qt/backend bridge]
    State --> Product[product_core: catalogue and stock]
    State --> Adapter[integration_core: ProductAdapter]
    Adapter --> Order[order_core: cart and order records]
    State --> Billing[billing_core: BillingService]
    Billing --> Order
    Billing --> Common[common_core: record encoding and money helpers]
    UI --> Camera[Optional scanner_core: OpenCV and EAN-8 decoder]
```

## Responsibilities

| Component | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `product_core` | Parse and validate catalogue records; lookup, descriptions and stock operations |
| `order_core` | Validate cart snapshots; generate order IDs; store headers and details |
| `integration_core` | Convert a `Product` into the order module's `ProductSnapshot` |
| `billing_core` | Calculate totals, select payment strategy, create and persist bills |
| `common_core` | Encode text fields, format money/quantities, split billing records |
| `barcode_ui` | Pages, dialogs, styling, state cache and checkout coordination |
| `scanner_core` | Read camera frames and decode full EAN-8 barcodes when enabled |

`backend_core` is a CMake interface target that collects backend dependencies.
Product lookup is injected into `BillingService`; billing does not directly own
the product catalogue. Payment strategies and product validation rules have
extension interfaces. Persistence uses local text files, with no database server.

## Active versus historical files

- `BarcodeHub` starts at [Frontend/src/main.cpp](../../Frontend/src/main.cpp).
- Main POS camera capture uses `CameraFeedWidget` in [Widgets.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/Widgets.cpp).
- `CameraDemo`, when enabled, uses [tests/camera_demo.cpp](../../tests/camera_demo.cpp)
  and `Frontend/src/ui/CameraWidget.cpp`.
- Root `cameraScanner.cpp`, `cameraScanner.h`, and `decoder.cpp` are not the
  sources selected for `scanner_core`; the backend scanner directory is used.
- `Frontend/src/ui/main.cpp`, `Frontend/legacy-camera`, and empty `POSWindow`
  files are outside the main executable's active implementation.
- `tests/regression.cpp` references old interfaces and is not registered by CMake.

The diagram describes source structure. It does not establish GUI readiness;
the checked-in frontend has a [confirmed compilation blocker](limitations.md).

FILE: /docs/increment-2/billing.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Billing calculations and service

Billing separates domain data, arithmetic, payment rules, serialization, storage,
and checkout coordination. Its backend uses standard C++17 without Qt headers.

## Responsibilities

| Component | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| [`Bill`](../../Backend/include/billing/Bill.h) | Header, item lines, and aggregate totals. |
| [`BillCalculator`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillCalculator.cpp) | Arithmetic and input checks. |
| [`BillingService`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillingService.cpp) | Product lookup, payment, status, and save sequence. |
| [`BillRepository`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp) | Load, group, append, and allocate bill IDs. |
| [`BillRecordCodec`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillRecordCodec.cpp) | Translate one item row to/from text. |

## Calculation rules

Tax and discount are PKR amounts per unit, not percentages.

```text
withoutTax = round(sum(unitPrice * quantity), 2)
tax        = round(sum(taxPerUnit * quantity), 2)
discount   = round(sum(discountPerUnit * quantity), 2)
payable    = round(withoutTax + tax - discount, 2)
```

Components are accumulated before rounding; they are not rounded per item first.
The implementation uses `double` and `std::round`, rather than integer minor units.
It rejects empty bills, nonfinite or negative price/tax/discount inputs,
nonpositive quantities, and a negative resulting payable amount.

For two units priced at PKR 250, with PKR 12.50 tax and PKR 10 discount per unit:

| Component | Calculation | PKR |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Subtotal | 250 x 2 | 500.00 |
| Tax | 12.50 x 2 | 25.00 |
| Discount | 10 x 2 | 20.00 |
| Payable | 500 + 25 - 20 | 505.00 |

## Preview and saved bills

`preview(orderId)` loads order details and resolves current product metadata
through an injected `ProductLookup`; it does not save a bill or assign a bill ID.
Checkout adds payment validation and persistence, rejecting paid/archived orders.
Bill IDs use `YYMMDD` + sequence `001..999`, for example `261119001`.
The daily sequence is selected from existing bill IDs using the local date.

Saved rows retain item price/discount/quantity and aggregate tax; per-item tax is
not stored. Rebuilding a historical preview from current product data can therefore
differ from the saved bill. A zero-total bill passes the calculator but cannot be
marked paid because order validation requires a positive payment amount.

[`billing_tests.cpp`](../../tests/billing_tests.cpp) contains 19 cases, while
[`checkout_test.cpp`](../../tests/checkout_test.cpp) checks cash checkout end to end.
All five registered backend CTest targets passed during this inspection.
See [payments](payments.md), [records](records.md), and [checkout](checkout.md).


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILE: /docs/increment-2/build.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Build, data location and packaging

Run commands from the repository root. CMake 3.21+, a C++17 compiler and Ninja
are needed for the commands below. The verified Windows backend compiler was
GNU 13.1.0. See [verification](verification.md) for the actual results.

## Backend without Qt or OpenCV

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build/increment2-audit-backend -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DBACKEND_ONLY=ON -DCAMERA=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=ON
cmake --build build/increment2-audit-backend --parallel 4
ctest --test-dir build/increment2-audit-backend --output-on-failure
```

Use `CAMERA=OFF` explicitly: the default is `ON`, and OpenCV discovery happens
before CMake exits for `BACKEND_ONLY`. The registered tests are product, order,
integration, checkout and billing. Tests do not require a camera.

## Qt build diagnostic

Qt Widgets and a compatible compiler are required. These paths describe the
reviewed Windows installation; adjust them for another machine.

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build/increment2-audit-gui -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCAMERA=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=OFF -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH=C:/Qt/6.11.0/mingw_64 -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=C:/Qt/Tools/mingw1310_64/bin/g++.exe
cmake --build build/increment2-audit-gui --parallel 4
```

At the original baseline, configuration succeeded but compilation failed at
`RecordOrderPage.cpp` because member declarations differed from its header.
After the separate header repair at `883425c`, the same camera-disabled build
passed and linked `BarcodeHub.exe`. See [follow-up results](presentation/review-results.md).
Interactive launch and checkout were not tested.

`CAMERA=ON` requires OpenCV 4 components `core`, `imgproc`, `imgcodecs`, `videoio`.
`CAMERA=AUTO` searches optionally; `OFF` skips OpenCV. Camera-enabled builds also
have filename case inconsistencies on case-sensitive platforms; see [scanner](scanner.md).

## Data selection and preservation

[AppState::locateDataDir](../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp) first checks the
`BARCODEHUB_DATA` environment variable, then executable-relative `data` folders,
the working directory, and the compiled source directory. A candidate must
contain `Product.txt`; an invalid override falls through to other candidates.
Use a separate copy of the entire `data` directory for any future GUI rehearsal,
because a successful checkout persists inventory and transaction changes.

[cmake/seed-data.cmake](../../cmake/seed-data.cmake) copies only missing starter
files. Rebuilding does not intentionally reset an existing data directory.

[scripts/build-windows.ps1](../../scripts/build-windows.ps1) detects a Qt MinGW
kit, builds OpenCV when needed, builds/tests the app, deploys Qt and OpenCV DLLs,
and assembles `build/portable/BarcodeHub`. This full packaging path was inspected
but not executed in this review. Deployment targets such as `BarcodeHub_deploy`
are optional; an executable alone is not a verified portable package.

FILE: /docs/increment-2/cart-and-stock.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cart and inventory flow

This reference follows the inspected Increment 2 source and sample data.
The GUI and camera were not runtime-verified for this document.
The following presentation sequence is a rehearsal guide, not a recorded test result.

## Adding products

The [order page](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp) previews a matched product.
Typed lookup and Browse select it; Add to order adds one unit by default.
A camera scan can add automatically when the corresponding setting is enabled.
The product card disables Add to order for a product whose stock is zero.
Existing cart lines are identified by acronym/SKU and their quantities increase.
The page supports quantity changes, line removal, clearing, and custom items.

Before increasing quantity, [AppState](../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp) asks
the [stock validator](../../Backend/src/product/ProductValidation.cpp) to check
the amount already in the cart plus the requested addition.
The default policy disallows a request above available inventory.
Zero/negative additions, out-of-stock products, and insufficient stock have distinct messages.
Reducing a line below one unit removes it.

## Price calculation

For each line, gross price, tax, and discount are multiplied by quantity.
The payable total is gross minus discount plus tax.
Tax and discount values in the catalogue are amounts per unit in PKR.
The UI recomputes its display and checkout compares it with backend totals.

Using unchanged [sample stock](../../data/Product.txt):

| Selection | Gross | Discount | Tax | Payable |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Two milk units, barcode `40001029` | 680.00 | 20.00 | 0.00 | 660.00 |
| One bread, barcode `40002149` | 260.00 | 0.00 | 18.00 | 278.00 |
| Combined order | 940.00 | 20.00 | 18.00 | 938.00 |

Cash of PKR 1,000 for that total gives PKR 62 change.
Eggs (`40007359`) demonstrate the four-unit stock boundary.
Cola (`40011721`) demonstrates an unavailable sample product.

## Checkout and persistence

Checkout rebuilds the backend cart from current catalogue product snapshots.
It rechecks accumulated stock demand and payment before creating an order.
Then it captures the original product file, deducts stock, and saves inventory.
Billing creates the bill and completes payment/status persistence.
If saving stock or billing fails, the UI attempts inventory restoration and order removal.
Rollback failure can require manual attention; the files are not one database transaction.

## Scope and limitations

Cart edits alone do not reserve stock or write inventory to disk.
Custom items have no catalogue stock entry and bypass inventory deduction.
The frontend uses integer quantities, including products labelled `weight`.
The [order backend](../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp) separately supports fractional weight quantities.
The loader rejects duplicate IDs/barcodes but allows duplicate acronyms;
because the UI merges by acronym, catalogue acronyms should remain unique.
The inspected checkout flow does not provide multi-process inventory locking.
Use a disposable data copy when rehearsing checkout, since a successful sale changes saved stock.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILE: /docs/increment-2/checkout.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Checkout coordination and recovery

Checkout joins the product, order, and billing modules through
[`AppState::addOrder`](../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp).
The sequence below describes the implemented source path, not a live GUI test.

## Before persistence

1. Reject missing data services, an empty order, or an unsupported payment mode.
2. Rebuild the backend cart from current catalog snapshots and manual item data.
3. Validate quantities and aggregate stock deductions for catalog products.
4. Compare GUI totals with backend totals and apply the selected payment rules.

Manual lines receive `CUSTOM-<acronym>` IDs and exist only in the checkout lookup.
Their bill price/discount/quantity survive restart; their full description does not.
The current GUI uses integer quantities, even though backend orders support weight
fractions. See [orders](orders.md) for the cart contract.

## Write sequence

1. Create an in-progress order: persist details, then its header.
2. Read the original `Product.txt` bytes for a possible stock restoration.
3. Deduct catalog stock in memory and save `Product.txt`.
4. Call [`BillingService::checkout`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillingService.cpp).
5. Revalidate order/payment, calculate the bill, and allocate a nine-digit bill ID.
6. Update the order to paid, then append the bill through `BillRepository`.
7. Refresh cached stock/history and return the saved IDs/totals to the GUI.

[`RecordOrderPage`](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp) displays a receipt
and clears its visible cart only after `addOrder` succeeds. A failed checkout
displays its error and retains the visible cart for correction or retry.

## Recovery paths

| Failure | Recovery attempted |
| --- | --- |
| Order header save after details | Restore the previous detail rows. |
| Product backup cannot be read | Discard the new in-progress order. |
| Product save or billing fails | Restore original product bytes and discard the new order. |
| Bill append fails after marking paid | Billing first attempts to restore the previous order state. |

Stock restoration uses `QSaveFile`. Several backend writers use a temporary file,
rename it into place, and fall back to overwriting by copy if rename fails.
Some rollback errors are ignored; the GUI reports "rollback needs manual attention"
when its checked restoration/discard operations fail.
These are best-effort recovery steps, not a crash-safe transaction across files.
No transaction journal or multi-process lock coordinates the whole sequence.

## Verification and demonstration

All five registered backend CTest targets passed during this inspection.
[`checkout_test.cpp`](../../tests/checkout_test.cpp) checks cash settlement, change,
insufficient tender, repeat-payment rejection, and saved order/bill reload.
It does not exercise the Qt stock orchestration or simulated disk/rollback failures.

For a later GUI rehearsal, use a disposable copy of the starter data. Two units of
barcode `40001029` should total PKR 660; tender PKR 700 returns PKR 40 change and
successful checkout reduces that product's stock from 42 to 40.
Inspect all four files afterward; preserve the original fixture for repeatable demos.

# Increment 2 presentation walkthrough - 20 November 2026

Present product lookup, scanning, order management, and billing as one workflow using four local text files.
The following GUI steps are a rehearsal plan, not a record of a successful live run.
The [presentation pack](presentation/README.md) adds viva questions and acceptance cases.

## Readiness gate

The original inspection found a frontend header/source mismatch in `RecordOrderPage`.
The separate header repair at `883425c` resolves that compile failure. A subsequent
Qt 6.11.0/GNU 13.1 build with `CAMERA=OFF` passed and linked `BarcodeHub.exe`.
No interactive GUI run followed. Rehearse checkout using copied data before the
presentation; camera capture requires a separately verified camera-enabled build.
See [follow-up results](presentation/review-results.md). This documentation work
did not make the source repair.

The backend audit passed all five CTest tests: product, order, integration, checkout,
and billing. That result covers those automated checks, not Qt screens or a camera.
If the GUI build remains blocked, use the backend evidence and source walkthrough.

## Manual-entry demonstration, after the gate passes

1. Open Products and identify milk `40001029` and bread `40002149`.
2. Open Details to show name, unit, price, tax, discount, stock, and barcode image.
3. Open Record Order. Replace the prefilled demo customer/contact values.
4. Type milk barcode `40001029`, press Enter, then click Add to order.
5. Increase milk quantity to two. Type bread `40002149` and explicitly add it once.
6. Explain the totals using the checked-in [product fixture](../../data/Product.txt).

| Item | Quantity | Unit price | Unit tax | Unit discount | Payable |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Olpers milk | 2 | 340 | 0 | 10 | 660 |
| National bread | 1 | 260 | 18 | 0 | 278 |
| Total (PKR) | 3 | | | | **938** |

Subtotal is PKR 940, discount PKR 20, tax PKR 18, and payable PKR 938.
Use current product values if the runtime data differs from this fixture.

7. Open Checkout. Show that insufficient cash disables confirmation.
8. Enter PKR 1,000, explain PKR 62 change, and confirm only in disposable demo data.
9. Show the receipt, then locate the saved order and its real bill ID in Orders/Bills.
10. Show the stock reduction and updated Dashboard totals for today's transaction.

## Additional talking points and fallbacks

- Card/Wallet require a merchant name and record payment locally; no bank is contacted.
- Show zero-stock Cola `40011721` in Products and explain why adding is disabled.
- Camera: stop the other page's capture, start one camera, and present a sample barcode.
- Camera fallback: type the barcode; manual entry exercises the same lookup/cart flow.
- Auto-add is off by default; explicit Add avoids confusing a preview with a cart line.
- Receipt is a text viewer; do not promise printing, PDF export, refunds, or draft resume.

For a backend-only fallback, show the CTest summary and explain the assertions in
[checkout tests](../../tests/checkout_test.cpp) and [billing tests](../../tests/billing_tests.cpp).
These commands passed all five tests in 4.22 seconds on the audit's Windows toolchain:

```powershell
cmake -S . -B build/increment2-audit-backend -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DBACKEND_ONLY=ON -DCAMERA=OFF -DBUILD_TESTING=ON
cmake --build build/increment2-audit-backend --parallel 4
ctest --test-dir build/increment2-audit-backend --output-on-failure
```

Show the [build definitions](../../CMakeLists.txt) to distinguish backend tests from Qt.
Use the [history guide](history.md) and [settings guide](settings.md) for scope questions.

Checkout writes inventory and order/billing records; use a separate data copy for rehearsal.

# Orders, bills, and dashboard

This guide describes the inspected Increment 2 implementation.
GUI behavior still needs a successful frontend build and interactive verification.
See the [presentation walkthrough](demo.md) for the build limitation and demo order.

## Orders and bills

- Orders loads persisted records through `AppState::refreshOrders()`.
- Rows are sorted newest first, with customer, contact, items, payment, and status.
- Search matches order ID, customer name, or contact; a leading `#` is ignored.
- Tabs filter All, Paid, In Progress, and On Hold.
- View details opens an item table; Receipt opens the text receipt viewer.
- There is no connected hold, resume, archive, or refund action in these screens.
- Bills lists paid orders only when an actual saved bill ID is available.
- Bill search accepts bill ID, order ID, or customer name.

The active cart is an in-memory draft. Clearing it does not delete saved history.
Closing the application loses that draft after the exit confirmation.
New Draft and successful checkout retain the customer and contact fields.
Check those fields before starting the next customer's order.

## Receipt persistence

Receipt totals come from the saved bill when one exists.
Saved bill lines retain unit prices and discounts for historical receipts.
The viewer shows item quantities, subtotal, discount, tax, total, and payment mode.
An immediate cash receipt also shows tendered cash and change.

The receipt dialog is a text preview with a Close button; it has no print or PDF action.
Cash tendered/change are not restored when history is reloaded from disk.
Custom item descriptions are available during checkout, but after reload their saved
acronyms are used as names. Catalogue names are obtained from the current catalogue.

## Dashboard

Sales Today sums paid orders dated today; Orders counts all saved orders dated today.
The average divides today's paid revenue by today's number of paid orders.
Recent Orders shows the latest five saved orders across all dates.
Low Stock counts products at or below the configured threshold, including zero stock.
Hourly revenue groups today's paid orders from 08:00 through 20:00 inclusive.
Revenue outside those hours remains in Sales Today but is absent from that chart.
Pending currently counts every non-Paid status, so Archived is included too.
Daily totals are cached on load/checkout; there is no midnight refresh timer.

## CSV export

Orders exports all loaded orders, regardless of the current search or status tab.
Columns are order ID, time, customer, contact, items, payment, amount, and status.
Embedded quotation marks in text fields are not escaped, so such names can break CSV.
The export helper reports open failure silently and does not verify final write status.

## Source map

- [Orders and Bills screens](../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp)
- [History loading and totals](../../Frontend/src/ui/AppState.cpp)
- [Dashboard aggregation](../../Frontend/src/ui/DashboardPage.cpp)
- [Receipt text and viewer](../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp)

FILE: /docs/increment-2/limitations.md

# Known limitations at the inspection baseline

Reviewed at `2d3c58d` on 19 November 2026. Only the GUI build failure below was
compiler-reproduced; other findings are from source inspection unless stated.
The five backend CTest suites passed. No functional fixes are included.

## Historical presentation blocker - resolved at `883425c`

**At the original baseline the Qt GUI did not compile.**
[RecordOrderPage.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp) used members
absent from [RecordOrderPage.h](../../Frontend/include/ui/RecordOrderPage.h).
A separate commit, `883425c`, restored the declarations. A subsequent Qt build
with `CAMERA=OFF` passed. See [follow-up results](presentation/review-results.md).
The source fix was not part of the documentation changes. Camera support and
interactive checkout still require runtime verification.

## Functional boundaries

| Finding | Consequence / source |
| --- | --- |
| Card/wallet validate merchant text locally | No bank authorization or settlement; [PaymentMethod.cpp](../../Backend/src/billing/PaymentMethod.cpp) |
| UI quantities and catalogue stock are integers | Backend fractional `weight` support is not available end-to-end in the GUI; [AppState.h](../../Frontend/include/ui/AppState.h) |
| Products/Scanner Add routes use the scan path | With auto-add disabled, they preview; use the Record Order Add button; [MainWindow.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp) |
| No persisted draft/hold/resume UI | Cart is memory-only, despite backend order states; [RecordOrderPage.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp) |
| Receipt is a text dialog | No implemented printer/PDF export; [Dialogs.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp) |
| Settings mostly last only for the session | Dark mode uses QSettings; beep flag has no scan action; [OtherPages.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp) |
| Navigation does not stop a running camera | Stop one page's camera before opening the other; [MainWindow.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp) |
| Daily chart covers 08:00-20:00 | Other-hour sales are outside the chart; Pending also includes archived orders; [DashboardPage.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/DashboardPage.cpp) |

## Data and edge cases

- Catalogue parsing accepts some non-finite numeric strings: `strtod` and the
  price rule lack a finite-value check. Later order validation rejects them.
  See [ProductStorage.cpp](../../Backend/src/product/ProductStorage.cpp) and
  [ProductValidation.cpp](../../Backend/src/product/ProductValidation.cpp).
- IDs/barcodes must be unique, but acronyms are not checked for uniqueness.
  The GUI merges cart rows by acronym; duplicate acronyms can combine distinct
  products. The shipped fixture uses distinct acronyms.
- A zero-payable bill can pass calculation/payment validation, but a Paid order
  requires positive payment, so checkout fails. See [Order.cpp](../../Backend/src/order/Order.cpp).
- Stock, order, detail and bill files are updated separately. Temporary-file
  replacement and rollback provide best-effort recovery, not a crash-safe
  multi-file transaction. No process locking exists; see [checkout.md](checkout.md).
- Bill parsing accepts finite amounts without verifying all business constraints
  or repeated header consistency. Per-line tax and complete historical display
  names are not snapshotted; see [records.md](records.md) and [history.md](history.md).
- CSV export wraps fields in quotes without escaping embedded quotes, which can
  produce malformed CSV. See [OtherPages.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp).

## Build and documentation differences

The camera source/header capitalization differs between Git filenames and some
includes/CMake paths, which can fail on case-sensitive systems. The main POS
does not expose the historical barcode generator or image-upload screens.
The product acronym rule defaults to 10 characters although the increment plan
specifies 8. Older product/billing README claims include obsolete paths and
overstated persistence guarantees; these inspection notes describe current code.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILE: /docs/increment-2/orders.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Order management

The order module owns cart rules, order identities, and order/detail persistence.
It is standard C++17 and does not depend on Qt or the product implementation.

## Cart contract

[`ProductSnapshot`](../../Backend/include/order/OrderManager.h) carries the product
ID, barcode, description, unit, pricing, and available quantity into this module.
[`ProductAdapter`](../../Backend/src/integration/ProductAdapter.cpp) builds that
snapshot from a catalog product.

- Adding the same product ID combines its quantity with the existing cart line.
- Quantities must be finite, positive, and within the snapshot's available stock.
- `count` requires whole quantities; `weight` permits fractions in this backend.
- Price, tax, and discount must be finite and nonnegative.
- Discount per unit cannot exceed price plus tax.
- Removing a line and clearing the cart are separate operations.

The active GUI uses integer quantities. Backend support for weighted fractions
does not imply fractional checkout support throughout the application.

## Saving an order

[`createOrder`](../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp) rejects an empty cart
or empty customer name, loads existing records, and allocates an ID at save time.
It writes `OrderDetail.txt` before `Order.txt`, attempts to restore the previous
details if the header write fails, and clears the cart only after success.
Creating an order alone neither charges a payment nor deducts product stock.

Order IDs have eight digits: `YYMM` + sequence `01..99` + `DD`.
For example, `26110119` is the first order on 19 November 2026.
The sequence is one above the highest existing ID for that date; 99 is the limit.
`nextOrderId()` is only a preview, not a reservation.

## Status and update rules

| Status | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `in-progress` | Initial persisted state; eligible for discard after failed checkout. |
| `on-hold` | Recognized persisted state; billing can settle it. |
| `paid` | Requires a positive payment amount; billing rejects another checkout. |
| `archived` | Cannot be reopened through `updateOrder`; billing rejects checkout. |

`updateOrder` preserves the original creation date and time. Beyond the archived
restriction and record validation, it is not a complete transition state machine.
Status support in the backend does not establish a working GUI action for each state.

## Verification and limits

[`OrderManagementSmokeTest`](../../tests/OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp) covers cart
validation, totals, IDs, escaping, persistence, updates, and malformed records.
The inspection run passed all five registered backend CTest targets.
Order dates/times are shape-checked; calendar validity is not fully checked.
Multi-file recovery is best effort; see [checkout](checkout.md) and [records](records.md).


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILE: /docs/increment-2/payments.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Payment modes

Payment handling records the cashier's selected mode and applies local validation.
Card and wallet support does not connect to a bank, terminal, or payment gateway.

## Strategy design

[`IPaymentMethod`](../../Backend/include/billing/PaymentMethod.h) defines `mode()`
and `process(request, payable)`. `PaymentMethodRegistry::withDefaults()` installs
cash, card, and wallet strategies; the service finds one using `PaymentMode`.

`PaymentRequest` contains a mode, merchant name, and cash amount tendered.
`PaymentResult` returns success, change, and an error message.
This separates payment rules from bill calculation and storage.
Adding a new mode also needs the enum/string mappings and any relevant UI changes;
implementing and registering a strategy alone does not add a new UI option.

## Current rules

| Mode | Required information | Behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cash | Finite, nonnegative tender covering payable | Returns change rounded to two decimals. |
| Card | Nonblank bank name without `#` | Records local acceptance; no remote authorization. |
| Wallet | Nonblank provider name without `#` | Records local acceptance; no remote authorization. |

For payable PKR 505 and tender PKR 510, cash returns PKR 5 change.
Tendering PKR 500 is rejected; negative or nonfinite tender is also rejected.
Electronic strategies ignore the tendered amount and return zero change.
Merchant validation rejects names made only of spaces, tabs, CR, or LF.

[`PaymentMethod.cpp`](../../Backend/src/billing/PaymentMethod.cpp) implements these
rules. Strategies assume the payable amount came from the billing calculation;
they do not each independently validate every possible payable input.

## GUI and persistence path

The payment dialog offers Cash, Card, and Wallet, showing either tender/change or
a bank/provider field. Cancel returns to the cart without committing checkout.
The dialog trims electronic merchant names before handing them to the backend.
These behaviors were inspected in [`Dialogs.cpp`](../../Frontend/src/ui/Dialogs.cpp);
they are not a claim of a live GUI test.

Successful billing writes the payable amount, mode, and merchant to `Order.txt`.
Cash saves an empty merchant. Tendered cash and change are not durable fields in
the order/bill schemas, so those values cannot be reconstructed from them alone.
`Bill.txt` stores the resulting payment status, not card or wallet credentials.

## Verification

[`billing_tests.cpp`](../../tests/billing_tests.cpp) checks exact cash, change,
insufficient/negative cash, valid and invalid card merchants, wallet acceptance,
and the default registry. The checkout test also verifies insufficient cash does
not complete payment and an already-paid order cannot be checked out again.
All five registered backend CTest targets passed during this inspection.
See [checkout](checkout.md) for write ordering and recovery limitations.


--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILE: /docs/increment-2/products.md
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Product catalogue reference

This reference describes behavior found by inspecting the Increment 2 source.
It does not establish that the GUI or camera has been run successfully.

## Responsibilities and source map

| Component | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| [ProductModel](../../Backend/include/product/ProductModel.h) | Product values, EAN-8 helpers, PKR text, descriptions |
| [ProductValidation](../../Backend/src/product/ProductValidation.cpp) | Composable validation rules and stock policies |
| [ProductStorage](../../Backend/src/product/ProductStorage.cpp) | Parsing, loading, catalogue searches, saving |
| [ProductAdapter](../../Backend/src/integration/ProductAdapter.cpp) | Copy product values into an order snapshot |

The product module uses standard C++17 and does not depend on Qt.
`ProductManager` owns catalogue products and indexes them by barcode and ID.
Its stock deduction changes memory; `saveProducts()` persists those values.

## Product.txt records

The active parser expects the following logical fields, ending in `#`:

```text
id barcode acronym name unit price tax discount stock barcodePath#
```

Names may contain spaces: the parser reads the final six fields from the right.
Barcode paths remain single tokens; paths containing spaces are unsupported.
Units are the lowercase words `count` and `weight`; stock is an integer.
Tax and discount are PKR amounts per unit, not percentage rates.
The first-line UTF-8 BOM and blank lines are handled by the file loader.

Malformed records are rejected individually, with line-numbered issues.
Valid records survive other rejected rows; duplicate IDs/barcodes are rejected.
`loadProducts()` can return false while retaining the valid rows in memory.
An eight-digit barcode with a bad checksum is retained with a warning.

## Lookup and description

- `searchByBarcode()` performs an exact lookup of the full barcode.
- `searchByID()` performs an integer ID lookup.
- `searchByCode()` tries barcode, numeric ID shorter than eight digits, then acronym.
- Acronym matching and text searches are case-insensitive.
- Text search covers name, acronym, and barcode; an empty query returns all products.
- Descriptions include stock status and `price - discount + tax` per unit.

The [sample catalogue](../../data/Product.txt) contains 14 product rows.
A read-only inspection found 14 valid EAN-8 checksums and 14 barcode PNG files.
For a presentation, `40001029`, `olpmilk`, and `1` select the same milk product.
Eggs (`40007359`) have stock 4; cola (`40011721`) has stock 0 in the sample.

## Boundaries to explain accurately

The implemented acronym limit is 10, although the increment plan specifies 8.
The loader does not reject duplicate acronyms; keep sample acronyms unique.
Price parsing/validation does not exclude every nonfinite numeric value;
the order layer separately rejects nonfinite amounts before adding them.
The writer uses a temporary file with a replacement fallback, not a database transaction.
The older strict `ProductRecordCodec` accepts exactly ten tokens and differs from the active parser.
See [product tests](../../tests/product_tests.cpp) for existing parser, search, and stock cases.

# Increment 2 inspection and presentation guide

Reviewed on 19 November 2026 against branch `ali`, starting at `2d3c58d`.
These notes describe the checked-in implementation and its verification results;
older planning documents describe intended requirements and can differ from the code.

**Latest readiness, 19 November:** all five backend CTest suites passed again.
The Qt frontend now compiles with `CAMERA=OFF` after the independently committed
header repair at `883425c`. Camera capture and the live checkout UI were not
tested. See the [follow-up results](presentation/review-results.md) and the
[presentation preparation pack](presentation/README.md) for the current evidence,
viva questions, requirements matrix, and pending rehearsal cases.

# Persistent text records

The application uses four local text files. Records are whitespace-delimited and
end with `#`; blank lines are skipped by the normal loaders.
The layouts below name logical fields in their exact stored order.

## Product.txt

```text
productId barcode acronym productName unit unitPrice taxPerUnit discountPerUnit availableQuantity barcodePath#
```

The active [`ProductParser`](../../Backend/src/product/ProductStorage.cpp) accepts
a multiword name by reading the six trailing fields from the right. Quantity is
an integer, including for a `weight` product. The barcode path is one token.
The separate `ProductRecordCodec` requires exactly ten tokens; it does not share
the active parser's multiword-name behavior. Product names are not percent-encoded.

## Order.txt and OrderDetail.txt

```text
orderId date time customerName customerContact status paymentAmount paymentMode paymentMerchant #
orderId productId productBarcode quantity #
```

The first layout belongs to `Order.txt`; the second belongs to `OrderDetail.txt`.
Orders require nine fields, details four. Orders have eight-digit IDs using
`YYMM` + daily sequence `01..99` + `DD`; dates/times use `YYYY-MM-DD`/`HH:MM:SS`.
Details save product identity and quantity, not a historical price snapshot.
[`OrderManager`](../../Backend/src/order/OrderManager.cpp) formats money to two
decimals and quantity to three decimals with trailing zeros removed.

## Bill.txt

```text
billId customerName orderId productAcronym unitPrice discountPerUnit quantity withoutTax totalTax totalDiscount payable paymentStatus #
```

There are twelve fields and a terminator. Each item occupies one row; all rows of
one bill repeat its ID, customer, order, aggregate totals, and status.
Bill IDs use `YYMMDD` + daily sequence `001..999`.
[`BillRepository`](../../Backend/src/billing/BillRepository.cpp) groups rows by ID.
The schema saves total tax but omits per-item tax, cash tender, and change.

## Text escaping and validation limits

Order text fields and bill customer/acronym/status fields use the same convention:
space becomes `_`, literal `_` becomes `%5F`, `%` becomes `%25`, and `#` becomes
`%23`. Empty text is `-`; a literal `-` is `%2D`. Tabs/newlines normalize to spaces
on round trip. See [`RecordText`](../../Backend/src/common/RecordText.cpp).

Order loading checks field count, numbers, enums, record validity, and duplicate IDs.
Bill parsing checks its terminator, field count, nine-digit bill ID, encoding, and
finite numbers; it does not fully check statuses, amount signs, order IDs, or totals.
Grouping bill rows does not check that repeated headers agree.
Temporary-file writes and recovery do not form a transaction across all four files.
There is no multi-process locking; avoid concurrent writers and keep data backups.

# Camera and EAN-8 scanner reference

This is a source-inspection reference for the Increment 2 camera path.
Camera hardware and GUI behavior were not runtime-verified for this document.
The current GUI compile check reports a header mismatch; see [verification](verification.md).
Rehearse using a verified build before presenting a live camera demonstration.

## Active source path

The [CMake configuration](../../CMakeLists.txt) selects the backend scanner library.
Its implementation is [CameraScanner.cpp](../../Backend/src/scanner/CameraScanner.cpp)
with [decoder.cpp](../../Backend/src/scanner/decoder.cpp).
The main POS embeds `CameraFeedWidget` from [Widgets.cpp](../../Frontend/src/ui/Widgets.cpp).
Root-level scanner copies and `Frontend/legacy-camera` are separate older sources.
The optional `CameraDemo` executable uses another `CameraWidget` implementation.
Do not infer main-POS image-upload features from the legacy camera widget.

## Frame-to-barcode processing

1. Open camera index 0 by default; Windows requests the DirectShow backend.
2. Request MJPG frames at 640 by 480 and 30 frames per second.
3. Convert a supported color frame to grayscale, or reuse a grayscale frame.
4. Attempt decoding across several thin horizontal bands.
5. If necessary, locate a likely barcode using gradients and morphology, then crop it.
6. When no suitable contour is found, also try a 90-degree rotation.

Requested camera settings depend on what the device actually supports.
The decoder measures 43 alternating stripe runs and reconstructs 67 modules.
It validates EAN-8 guard patterns, matches eight digits, and checks the checksum.
It also tries reversing the bit sequence for the opposite orientation.
The historical method name `decodeSix()` returns the **full eight-digit EAN-8**.
This matches the catalogue barcode keys rather than a six-digit payload.

## Main POS integration

`CameraFeedWidget` requests a frame on a 40 ms Qt timer.
The same detected code is suppressed until more than three seconds have elapsed;
a different code can be emitted immediately.
Stopping the main feed destroys its scanner and releases the camera.
Read failures stop the feed and emit an error message.
The [order page](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp) looks up the detected code.
Automatic addition to the cart depends on the `autoAddScans` setting.
Typed barcode, ID/acronym lookup, and Browse provide independent catalogue entry paths.

## Presentation limits

Camera builds require OpenCV 4; builds without it report disabled camera support.
This decoder implements EAN-8, not a general QR or every-barcode-format scanner.
No automated camera/decoder test is registered in the current CTest configuration.
The old `tests/regression.cpp` references different APIs and is not a current CTest target.
Source/header capitalization differs in some CMake/include references; verify the build environment.
Do not claim arbitrary image rotation, hardware compatibility, or successful live scanning without rehearsal.
For fallback, enter sample barcode `40001029` and explain the same product-lookup boundary.

# Navigation, scanning preferences, and settings

The application starts on Record Order and provides seven sidebar screens.
These notes describe source behavior; see the [demo readiness gate](demo.md).

## Keyboard navigation

| Key | Current action |
| --- | --- |
| F1 | Open Dashboard. |
| F2 | Open Record Order and focus barcode input. |
| F3 | Open Orders. |
| F4 | Browse products on Record Order; otherwise open Products. |
| F5 | Open Barcode Scanner. |
| F6 | Open Bills. |
| F9 | Open Record Order if needed and start its camera. |
| F10 | Checkout on Record Order; otherwise open Settings. |
| Ctrl+K | Open Dashboard and focus its lookup field. |
| Ctrl+N | Open Record Order and clear the current cart. |

F4 does not open Product Description, despite the current header comment.
Use the visible Description or Details button to inspect a product.
The Scanner screen's Start Scan button starts that screen's camera;
its F9 label differs from the global shortcut, which routes to Record Order.
Dashboard New Order navigates to the draft without clearing an existing cart.

## Apply settings

| Setting | Effect | Persistence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dark mode | Reapplies the application stylesheet. | Saved through `QSettings`. |
| Low-stock limit | Changes stock badges and low-stock counts. | Current process only. |
| Auto-add scanned items | Adds a detected product to the cart when enabled. | Current process only. |
| Beep on successful scan | Checkbox value is stored but no sound is implemented. | Current process only. |

Defaults are a low-stock limit of 10, auto-add off, and beep enabled.
The initial dark-mode value is read from `BarcodeHub/SmartPOS` settings.
Tax and discount are per-product PKR amounts loaded from `Product.txt`.
There is no global tax/discount editor in Settings.

## Scanning and explicit Add actions

Manual barcode entry and Browse show a preview; use Add to order to add the item.
With auto-add off, Products Add and Scanner Add last scan to order also only
open the Record Order preview because they share the scanner-style entry path.
With auto-add enabled, that entry path adds one unit after a successful lookup.
Stock validation still applies to additions and quantity increases.

The two camera pages own separate camera widgets and keep running when hidden.
Stop capture before switching to the other camera page to avoid device contention.
Holding the same barcode in view can emit it again after roughly three seconds;
with auto-add enabled, keep the cart quantity visible during a demonstration.

## Source map

- [Navigation and shortcuts](../../Frontend/src/ui/MainWindow.cpp)
- [Settings screen](../../Frontend/src/ui/OtherPages.cpp)
- [Defaults](../../Frontend/include/ui/AppState.h) and [theme persistence](../../Frontend/src/ui/Theme.cpp)
- [Cart entry behavior](../../Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp)
- [Camera lifecycle and duplicate suppression](../../Frontend/src/ui/Widgets.cpp)

# Verification record - 19 November 2026

**Follow-up:** the original results below are historical. The separate header
repair at `883425c` resolves the reported compilation errors; a camera-disabled
Qt rebuild now passes. The backend rerun also passed 5/5 in 2.20 seconds.
See [follow-up results](presentation/review-results.md) for the current state.

Baseline: branch `ali`, commit `2d3c58d`. Review changes are Markdown documentation
only; executable source, tests, build configuration and data remain unchanged.

## Executed checks

| Check | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Fetch and fast-forward pull of `origin/ali` | Already up to date |
| Compare baseline with fetched `origin/main` | 17 commits ahead, 0 behind |
| Fresh backend Debug configure/build | Passed with GNU 13.1.0 and Ninja on Windows |
| Registered CTest suites | 5/5 passed; 4.22 seconds total |
| Fresh Qt 6.11.0 configure, camera disabled | Passed with Qt's matching MinGW 13.1 compiler |
| Qt frontend compilation | Failed: undeclared members in `RecordOrderPage.cpp` |
| Catalogue fixture inspection | 14 records, 14 valid EAN-8 checksums, 14 matching PNG files |

Exact configure/build/test commands are in [build.md](build.md).

## What the passing tests establish

| CTest name | Source and coverage |
| --- | --- |
| `product` | [product_tests.cpp](../../tests/product_tests.cpp): parsing, validation, lookup, stock, fixture loading and persistence checks |
| `order` | [OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp](../../tests/OrderManagementSmokeTest.cpp): quantities, totals, IDs, record encoding, states and malformed records |
| `integration` | [integration_test.cpp](../../tests/integration_test.cpp): typed barcode lookup, product adapter, cart, order files and in-memory stock deduction |
| `checkout` | [checkout_test.cpp](../../tests/checkout_test.cpp): bill preview, insufficient cash, change, duplicate payment rejection and stored order/bill |
| `billing` | [billing_tests.cpp](../../tests/billing_tests.cpp): 19 cases for arithmetic, payment methods, codec and repository |

Integration and product tests depend on the shipped 14-item catalogue. Order,
integration, checkout and billing tests use temporary transaction directories.
No application checkout was executed against the repository's data files.

## Build failure evidence

The compiler reported at `Frontend/src/ui/RecordOrderPage.cpp:61`:

```text
error: 'm_feed' was not declared in this scope
```

Additional errors include `m_customer`, `m_contact`, `m_manual`, `m_sessionId`,
`m_checkout` and total-label members. The corresponding declarations in
[RecordOrderPage.h](../../Frontend/include/ui/RecordOrderPage.h) use stale names.
This failure exists before the documentation changes and was not repaired.

## Not verified

Live GUI interaction, hardware camera capture, image decoding quality, portable
deployment, receipt printing, power-loss recovery and concurrent application
instances were not tested. Backend CTest does not execute `AppState::addOrder`
or the Qt widgets. `tests/regression.cpp` and `CameraDemo` are not registered
CTest suites; their presence is not evidence of a passing current GUI test.

FILE: /Frontend/include/ui/AppState.h

#pragma once
// Qt-facing bridge to the Product, Order, and Billing backend modules.
// Data is loaded from and persisted to Product.txt, Order.txt, OrderDetail.txt and Bill.txt.
#include "product/ProductModel.h"
#include "product/ProductStorage.h"
#include "product/ProductValidation.h"
#include "order/OrderManager.h"
#include "billing/BillRepository.h"
#include <memory>
#include <QDateTime>
#include <QObject>
#include <QPair>
#include <QSet>
#include <QString>
#include <QVector>
#include <optional>

struct ProductInfo
{
    int id = 0; // 0 = custom (manual) line, not in the catalogue
    QString sku;   // acronym (max 8 chars)
    QString ean;   // barcode
    QString name;
    QString unit;  // "count" | "weight"
    double price = 0;    // unit price, no tax
    double tax = 0;      // tax per unit (PKR)
    double discount = 0; // discount per unit (PKR)
    int stock = 0;
    QString imagePath;   // resolved barcode image (may not exist)
    static ProductInfo from(const Product &p, const QString &rootDir);
};

struct ProductDescriptionView
{
    QString title, level; // level: in-stock | low | out
    QVector<QPair<QString, QString>> fields;
    QString imagePath;
};

struct ReceiptLine
{
    QString name, acronym;
    int qty = 1;
    double price = 0;    // unit price, no tax
    double tax = 0;      // per unit
    double discount = 0; // per unit
    int productId = 0;   // 0 = manually entered product
};

struct OrderRecord
{
    QString id;
    QString billId; // only nonempty when an actual Bill.txt record exists
    QDateTime time;
    QString customer, contact, tier, payment, merchant, status, shift;
    QVector<ReceiptLine> lines;
    double subtotal = 0, discount = 0, tax = 0, total = 0; // total = subtotal - discount + tax
    double tendered = 0;
    int itemCount() const;
    void recompute();
    QString summary() const; // "3 items  Milk, Bread, +1"
};

struct Settings
{
    int lowStockThreshold = 10;
    bool autoAddScans = false;
    bool beepOnScan = true;
};

class AppState : public QObject
{
    Q_OBJECT
public:
    static AppState &instance();

    // ---- products (backed by Product.txt) ----
    bool loadProducts(const QString &path);
    QString productFile() const { return m_productPath; }
    QStringList productIssues() const; // "line 4: acronym ... " (errors and warnings)
    int productErrorCount() const { return m_manager.getLastLoad().errorCount(); }
    const QVector<ProductInfo> &products() const { return m_products; }
    std::optional<ProductInfo> findByCode(const QString &code) const; // barcode, id or acronym
    QVector<ProductInfo> searchText(const QString &query) const;
    ProductDescriptionView describe(int productId) const;
    // Empty when `toAdd` more units may be added; otherwise the reason (from the StockValidator).
    QString stockProblem(int productId, int alreadyInOrder, int toAdd) const;
    QVector<ProductInfo> lowStock() const;
    bool isReordered(int productId) const { return m_reordered.contains(productId); }
    void reorder(int productId);
    void reorderAllLow();
    static QString locateDataDir();

    // ---- orders ----
    const QVector<OrderRecord> &orders() const { return m_orders; } // newest first
    QString nextOrderId();
    bool addOrder(OrderRecord &o, QString *error = nullptr); // creates order, bill, updates stock; reports failures
    double salesToday() const { return m_baseSales + m_sessionSales; }
    int ordersToday() const { return m_baseOrders + m_sessionOrders; }

    Settings &settings() { return m_settings; }
    void emitChanged() { emit changed(); }
    void emitScan(const QString &code) { emit scanned(code); }

signals:
    void changed();
    void scanned(const QString &code);

private:
    AppState();
    void refreshCache();
    void refreshOrders();
    ProductManager m_manager;
    StockValidator m_stock;
    QVector<ProductInfo> m_products;
    QString m_root;
    QString m_productPath;
    std::unique_ptr<barcodehub::order::OrderManager> m_orderManager;
    std::unique_ptr<barcodehub::billing::BillRepository> m_billRepository;
    QVector<OrderRecord> m_orders;
    QSet<int> m_reordered;
    Settings m_settings;
    double m_baseSales = 0, m_sessionSales = 0;
    int m_baseOrders = 0, m_sessionOrders = 0;
};

FILE: /Frontend/include/ui/DarkModeManager.h

#pragma once

/**
 * @file DarkModeManager.h
 * @brief Self-contained Dark Mode subsystem and top-bar toggle component for BarcodeHub.
 * @author Ali Abdullah (25L-3022)
 *
 * Implements a standalone theme management module adhering to Separation of Concerns:
 * - Manages application-wide theme switching (Light <-> Dark mode) dynamically at runtime.
 * - Generates high-contrast, polished dark stylesheets tailored for Point of Sale terminals.
 * - Persists theme state across sessions via QSettings.
 * - Provides a ready-to-mount, interactive toggle button widget for the application top bar.
 */

#include <QObject>
#include <QPushButton>
#include <QIcon>
#include <QPixmap>
#include <QString>

class QWidget;

namespace barcodehub::ui {

/**
 * @class DarkModeManager
 * @brief Central controller managing application theme state, stylesheet injection, and persistence.
 */
class DarkModeManager : public QObject {
    Q_OBJECT

public:
    /**
     * @brief Accesses the singleton instance of the DarkModeManager.
     * @return Reference to the DarkModeManager instance.
     */
    static DarkModeManager& instance();

    /**
     * @brief Checks if Dark Mode is currently active.
     * @return True if dark mode is active, false if light mode.
     */
    bool isDarkMode() const;

    /**
     * @brief Sets the application theme mode and immediately reapplies the global stylesheet.
     * @param enable True to enable dark mode, false for light mode.
     */
    void setDarkMode(bool enable);

    /**
     * @brief Toggles between light and dark modes.
     */
    void toggle();

    /**
     * @brief Generates the comprehensive QSS stylesheet for the current active theme.
     * @return QString containing complete Qt stylesheet rules.
     */
    QString getActiveStyleSheet() const;

    /**
     * @brief Factory creating a styled, pre-wired toggle button for the application top bar.
     *
     * The button features dynamic icons (Sun for Light mode, Moon for Dark mode),
     * a pill-shaped modern layout, and tooltips. Clicking automatically calls toggle().
     *
     * @param parent Optional parent widget.
     * @return Pointer to the configured QPushButton.
     */
    QPushButton* createTopBarToggle(QWidget* parent = nullptr);

    /**
     * @brief Renders a vector sun icon using QPainter.
     * @param color Icon color.
     * @param size Pixel dimensions.
     * @return QIcon rendered with crisp antialiasing.
     */
    static QIcon createSunIcon(const QColor& color, int size = 20);

    /**
     * @brief Renders a vector moon icon using QPainter.
     * @param color Icon color.
     * @param size Pixel dimensions.
     * @return QIcon rendered with crisp antialiasing.
     */
    static QIcon createMoonIcon(const QColor& color, int size = 20);

signals:
    /**
     * @brief Emitted whenever the theme changes.
     * @param isDark True if switched to dark mode, false if light mode.
     */
    void themeChanged(bool isDark);

private:
    explicit DarkModeManager(QObject* parent = nullptr);
    ~DarkModeManager() override = default;
    DarkModeManager(const DarkModeManager&) = delete;
    DarkModeManager& operator=(const DarkModeManager&) = delete;

    void applyTheme();
    QString buildDarkPaletteStyleSheet() const;

    bool m_isDark{false};
};

} // namespace barcodehub::ui

#pragma once

/**
 * @file Dialogs.h
 * @brief Modal dialog windows and receipt generation utilities for the POS GUI.
 * @author Ali Abdullah (25L-3022)
 *
 * Implements the interactive dialog workflows assigned for Increment 2:
 * - Payment selection modal (supporting Cash, Card, and Mobile Wallet).
 * - Receipt viewer modal (displaying finalized bill details and printable format).
 * - Product Description modal (displaying specifications, stock, and barcode graphic).
 * - Order details inspector.
 */

#include "AppState.h"
#include "UserRole.h"
#include <QString>

class QWidget;

namespace Dialogs
{
    /**
     * @brief Launches the payment settlement dialog allowing the cashier to complete an order.
     *
     * Provides radio options for Cash, Card, and Wallet. Dynamically adjusts input fields
     * to accept tendered cash (calculating live change) or bank/merchant provider name.
     *
     * @param[in] parent Owning parent widget for modal centering.
     * @param[in] total Total bill amount payable in PKR.
     * @param[out] mode Selected payment method string ("Cash", "Card", "Wallet").
     * @param[out] tendered Physical currency amount entered if Cash mode was selected.
     * @param[out] merchant Bank or digital wallet identifier if Card/Wallet mode was selected.
     * @return True if customer confirmed payment; false if checkout was cancelled.
     */
    bool payment(QWidget *parent, double total, QString &mode, double &tendered, QString &merchant);

    /**
     * @brief Displays a clean printable-style receipt preview dialog for a finalized order.
     * @param parent Owning parent widget.
     * @param order Fully recorded order with items, customer details, and payment metadata.
     */
    void receipt(QWidget *parent, const OrderRecord &order);

    /**
     * @brief Displays detailed line items, timestamps, and customer metadata for an order.
     * @param parent Owning parent widget.
     * @param order Target OrderRecord to inspect.
     */
    void orderDetails(QWidget *parent, const OrderRecord &order);

    /**
     * @brief Displays the Product Description popup dialog.
     *
     * Fulfills Increment 2 requirement: Cashier can inspect comprehensive product
     * attributes (acronym, description, pricing, tax, discount, available inventory)
     * along with rendered barcode graphic.
     *
     * @param parent Owning parent widget.
     * @param productId Numeric identifier of the product to query.
     */
    void productDescription(QWidget *parent, int productId);

    /**
     * @brief Displays any syntax or data integrity issues detected during Product.txt parsing.
     * @param parent Owning parent widget.
     */
    void loadIssues(QWidget *parent);

    /**
     * @brief Generates formatted monospace text representation of an order's finalized bill receipt.
     *
     * Contains business header, itemized quantities, unit prices, discounts, taxes,
     * total payable, amount tendered, change returned, and payment method details.
     *
     * @param order Recorded order data.
     * @return Formatted multi-line receipt text.
     */
    QString receiptText(const OrderRecord &order);

    /** @brief The bill as formatted HTML (used on screen and for the PDF). */
    QString receiptHtml(const OrderRecord &order);

    /** @brief Saves the bill as a PDF file; returns false if it could not be written. */
    bool saveReceiptPdf(const OrderRecord &order, const QString &path);
    /**
     * @brief Sign-in screen: the user types a name and presses Cashier or Manager.
     * @param[out] role Chosen role.
     * @param[out] name Typed name (the role name if left empty).
     * @return False if the window was closed without signing in.
     */
    bool login(QWidget *parent, UserRole &role, QString &name);
}

#pragma once
#include <QMainWindow>
#include <QVector>
#include "UserRole.h"

class QLabel;
class QPushButton;
class QStackedWidget;
class QTimer;
class DashboardPage;
class RecordOrderPage;
class OrdersPage;
class ProductsPage;
class BillsPage;
class ScannerPage;
class SettingsPage;

// Application shell: sidebar navigation, top bar, page stack, F-key shortcuts and toasts.
class MainWindow : public QMainWindow
{
    Q_OBJECT
public:
    enum Page { Dashboard, RecordOrder, Orders, Products, Scanner, Bills, Settings, PageCount };
    explicit MainWindow(UserRole role = UserRole::Cashier, const QString &userName = QString(), QWidget *parent = nullptr);
    bool loggedOut() const { return m_loggedOut; } // true if closed with "Log out"
    bool goTo(int page); // false if the signed-in role may not open this page
    QPixmap grabPage(int page); // used by the screenshot/self-test helper

protected:
    void resizeEvent(QResizeEvent *e) override;
    void closeEvent(QCloseEvent *e) override;

private slots:
    void showToast(const QString &text);

private:
    QWidget *buildSidebar();
    QWidget *buildTopBar();
    void handleFKey(int n);
    void positionToast();

    UserRole m_role;
    QString m_userName;

/**
 * @file RecordOrderPage.h
 * @brief Primary interactive POS cashier screen for recording orders and completing checkout.
 * @author Ali Abdullah (25L-3022)
 *
 * Implements the core Point of Sale GUI workflow for Increment 2:
 * - Dual-pane layout: Camera scanner & manual barcode entry on the left, shopping cart & totals on the right.
 * - Keyboard shortcuts: F9 (Camera Scan), F4 (Product Description), F10 (Checkout), F2 (Focus Barcode Input).
 * - Real-time stock validation and dynamic bill subtotal calculation.
 * - Integration with Dialogs::payment() and Dialogs::receipt().
 */
