set(QT_INSTALL_ROOT "C:/Qt"
    CACHE PATH "Qt installation root"
)

if(WIN32 AND NOT CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH AND NOT Qt6_DIR)

    file(GLOB _qt_roots
        "${QT_INSTALL_ROOT}/6.*/mingw_64"
    )

    if(_qt_roots)
        list(SORT _qt_roots
            COMPARE NATURAL
            ORDER DESCENDING
        )

        list(GET _qt_roots 0 _qt_root)
        list(PREPEND CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH "${_qt_root}")
    endif()

endif()

set(CMAKE_AUTOMOC ON)
set(CMAKE_AUTOUIC ON)
set(CMAKE_AUTORCC ON)

find_package(QT NAMES Qt6 Qt5
    REQUIRED COMPONENTS Widgets
)

find_package(Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}
    REQUIRED COMPONENTS Widgets
)

# A MinGW build of Qt cannot be compiled with MSVC (this is what "Qt requires a C++17 compiler ...
# /Zc:__cplusplus" means: the kit picked cl.exe). Stop early with the fix instead of 100 errors.
if(MSVC AND "${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}" MATCHES "mingw")
    message(FATAL_ERROR
        "Qt is a MinGW build (${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}) but the compiler is MSVC (${CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER}).\n"
        "Fix: Qt Creator > Projects > Build & Run > use the kit 'Desktop Qt 6.x.x MinGW 64-bit' "
        "(its compiler must be 'MinGW ... 64-bit', not 'Microsoft Visual C++'), delete the build folder, "
        "and configure again. Or use the 'Desktop Qt 6.x.x MSVC2022 64-bit' kit instead.")
endif()

set(CAMERA_ENABLED OFF)
set(CAMERA_LIB "")
if(NOT CAMERA_MODE STREQUAL "OFF")
    if(NOT CAMERA_BACKEND STREQUAL "QT")
        # MinGW: first look for an OpenCV config built for MinGW (x64/mingw/lib), then for loose
        # headers + libopencv_*.dll files. (The official Windows download is MSVC-only.)
        if(MINGW)
            file(GLOB_RECURSE _mingw_cfg "$ENV{USERPROFILE}/Downloads/opencv/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake"
                                         "${OpenCV_DIR}/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake" "C:/opencv/*mingw*/OpenCVConfig.cmake")
            if(_mingw_cfg AND NOT OpenCV_DIR)
                list(GET _mingw_cfg 0 _first_cfg)
                get_filename_component(_cfg_dir "${_first_cfg}" DIRECTORY)
                set(OpenCV_DIR "${_cfg_dir}" CACHE PATH "OpenCV (MinGW)" FORCE)
            endif()
        endif()
        find_package(OpenCV 4 QUIET COMPONENTS core imgproc videoio)
        if(OpenCV_FOUND)
            set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
            set(CAMERA_LIB OPENCV)
            message(STATUS "Camera: OpenCV ${OpenCV_VERSION} (${OpenCV_DIR})")
        elseif(MINGW)
            list(APPEND CMAKE_MODULE_PATH "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake")
            include(FindMinGWOpenCV)
            if(MINGW_OPENCV_FOUND)
                set(OpenCV_INCLUDE_DIRS "${MINGW_OPENCV_INCLUDE}")
                set(OpenCV_LIBS ${MINGW_OPENCV_LIBS})
                set(OPENCV_DLL_DIR "${MINGW_OPENCV_DLL_DIR}")
                set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
                set(CAMERA_LIB OPENCV)
                message(STATUS "Camera: OpenCV (MinGW files found without a CMake config)")
            endif()
        endif()
    endif()
    if(NOT CAMERA_ENABLED AND NOT CAMERA_BACKEND STREQUAL "OPENCV" AND QT_VERSION_MAJOR EQUAL 6)
        find_package(Qt6 QUIET COMPONENTS Multimedia)
        if(TARGET Qt6::Multimedia)
            set(CAMERA_ENABLED ON)
            set(CAMERA_LIB QT)
            message(STATUS "Camera: Qt Multimedia ${Qt6Multimedia_VERSION}")
        endif()
    endif()
    if(NOT CAMERA_ENABLED)
        set(_why "No usable camera library: install Qt Multimedia (Qt Maintenance Tool > Add or remove components > "
                 "Additional Libraries > Qt Multimedia), or give OpenCV_DIR an OpenCV built for THIS compiler "
                 "(the official OpenCV Windows download only works with the MSVC kit, not MinGW).")
        if(CAMERA_MODE STREQUAL "ON")
            message(FATAL_ERROR ${_why})
        else()
            message(STATUS "Camera disabled. ${_why}")
        endif()
    endif()
endif()

# OpenCV camera build on Windows: copy the OpenCV runtime DLLs next to BarcodeHub.exe.
if(WIN32 AND CAMERA_LIB STREQUAL "OPENCV")
    file(GLOB _opencv_dlls
        "${OpenCV_DIR}/x64/vc16/bin/*.dll" "${OpenCV_DIR}/x64/vc17/bin/*.dll"
        "${OpenCV_DIR}/../bin/*.dll" "${OpenCV_DIR}/../../bin/*.dll"
        "${OPENCV_DLL_DIR}/*.dll" "${OPENCV_DLL_DIR}/../bin/*.dll")
    if(_opencv_dlls)
        add_custom_command(TARGET BarcodeHub POST_BUILD
            COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E copy_if_different ${_opencv_dlls} "$<TARGET_FILE_DIR:BarcodeHub>"
            VERBATIM COMMENT "Copying OpenCV runtime DLLs")
    else()
        message(WARNING "No OpenCV DLLs found near ${OpenCV_DIR}; add the OpenCV bin folder to PATH to run the exe.")
    endif()
endif()

# Preserve existing records during rebuilding.
# Only copy starter data files when they do not already exist.

add_custom_command(TARGET BarcodeHub POST_BUILD
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND}
        "-DSOURCE_DATA=${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/data"
        "-DDEST_DATA=$<TARGET_FILE_DIR:BarcodeHub>/data"
        -P "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake/seed-data.cmake"
    VERBATIM
)

# ============================================================
# WINDOWS DEPLOYMENT (OPTIONAL)
# ============================================================

# Deployment is not part of the normal build. Qt Creator sets up the
# Qt/MinGW environment when launching an application using its Qt kit.
# To copy Qt DLLs and plugins for standalone distribution, build the
# BarcodeHub_deploy target explicitly instead.

if(WIN32)
    # Prefer the deployment tool belonging to the Qt version used by this kit.
    if(TARGET Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}::windeployqt)
        get_target_property(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE
            Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}::windeployqt IMPORTED_LOCATION)
    endif()

    if(NOT WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE)
        find_program(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE
            NAMES windeployqt
            HINTS
                "${Qt${QT_VERSION_MAJOR}_DIR}/../../../bin"
                "${QT_INSTALL_ROOT}/6.11.0/mingw_64/bin"
        )
    endif()

    if(WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE)
        set(_deploy_apps BarcodeHub)
        if(TARGET CameraDemo)
            list(APPEND _deploy_apps CameraDemo)
        endif()

        foreach(app IN LISTS _deploy_apps)
            add_custom_target(${app}_deploy
                COMMAND "${WINDEPLOYQT_EXECUTABLE}"
                    --no-translations
                    "$<TARGET_FILE:${app}>"
                DEPENDS ${app}
                VERBATIM
                COMMENT "Deploying Qt runtime for ${app}"
            )
        endforeach()
    else()
        message(STATUS
            "windeployqt not found; optional deployment targets disabled")
    endif()
endif()

# Contributing Guidelines

Welcome to **BarcodeHub**! This document outlines the contribution workflow and development guidelines for all team members working on **Bar-Code-System-CPP**.

Please follow these guidelines to maintain a consistent and organized development workflow.

## 1. Clone the Repository

Clone the repository and navigate to the project directory:

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Syntrojex/Bar-Code-System-CPP.git
cd Bar-Code-System-CPP
```

## 2. Switch to Your Assigned Branch

Each team member has an assigned branch. Switch to your respective branch before making any changes.

```bash
git checkout your-branch
```

### Available Team Branches

| Team Member | Branch |
|---|---|
| Muhammad Mustafa Amir | `mustafa` |
| Ali Abdullah | `ali` |
| Farzam Zeeshan | `farzam` |
| Shauzab Mukhtar | `shauzab` |

**Important:** Always work on your assigned branch. Do not make changes directly on `main`.

## 3. Synchronize Your Branch

Before starting new work, make sure your local branch has the latest changes:

```bash
git pull origin your-branch
```

This helps prevent conflicts and keeps your local development environment up to date.

## 4. Make Your Changes

Implement the changes related to your assigned task.

Follow these practices:

- Maintain the existing project structure.
- Follow the established C++ coding conventions.
- Write clean, readable, and maintainable code.
- Avoid modifying unrelated files.
- Test your changes before committing.

## 5. Stage and Commit Changes

Review your modifications:

```bash
git status
```

Stage the relevant files:

```bash
git add .
```

Create a meaningful commit:

```bash
git commit -m "Add barcode generation functionality"
```

Keep commit messages concise and descriptive. Each commit should represent a meaningful change.

## 6. Push Your Changes

Push your commits to your assigned remote branch:

```bash
git push origin your-branch
```

## 7. Create a Pull Request

Once your changes have been pushed, create a Pull Request to propose merging them into `main`.

1. Open the project repository on GitHub.
2. Select **Compare & pull request**.
3. Set the base branch to `main`.
4. Select your assigned branch as the compare branch.
5. Provide a clear and descriptive title.
6. Explain the changes and their purpose in the description.
7. Create the Pull Request.

## 8. Review and Merge

All Pull Requests must go through the project's review process before being merged.

- The project lead reviews submitted Pull Requests.
- Address any requested changes before merging.
- Ensure the proposed changes do not introduce unnecessary modifications or conflicts.
- Only reviewed and approved contributions should be integrated into `main`.
- Team members must not push directly to `main`.

## 9. Automatic Branch Synchronization

The project uses a GitHub Actions workflow to automatically synchronize team branches with the latest `main` branch.

Whenever changes are pushed to `main`, the workflow:

- Detects existing team branches automatically.
- Attempts to merge the latest `main` changes into each branch.
- Pushes successfully synchronized branches back to GitHub.
- Reports branches that encounter merge conflicts or synchronization failures.

## General Guidelines

- Always work on your assigned branch.
- Keep commits meaningful and focused.
- Follow the established project structure and coding conventions.
- Test your changes before submitting a Pull Request.
- Keep Pull Requests limited to their intended tasks.
- Avoid committing generated build files and unnecessary dependencies.
- Communicate with the project lead when clarification is needed.
- Never commit directly to `main`.
---

Thank you for contributing to **BarcodeHub**!

FILE: /decoder.cpp
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#include <iostream>
#include <cmath>
#include <opencv2/imgproc.hpp>
#include "decoder.h"
using namespace std;
// patterns representing numbers 0-9
const char *const Decoder::patterns[10] = {"0001101", "0011001", "0010011", "0111101", "0100011",
                                           "0110001", "0101111", "0111011", "0110111", "0001011"};
// store weights at each line
std::vector<double> Decoder::scanLineRuns(const cv::Mat &barcode) const
{
    if (barcode.empty() || barcode.type() != CV_8UC1 || barcode.rows < 5)
    {
        return {};
    }
    int y0 = barcode.rows * 4 / 10;
    int y1 = barcode.rows * 6 / 10;
    cv::Mat line;
    cv::reduce(barcode.rowRange(y0, y1), line, 0, cv::REDUCE_AVG, CV_32F);
    double low, high;
    cv::minMaxLoc(line, &low, &high);
    float T = (float)((low + high) / 2);
    vector<double> edges;
    for (int i = 0; i + 1 < line.cols; i++)
    {
        float a = line.at<float>(0, i);
        float b = line.at<float>(0, i + 1);
        if ((a < T) != (b < T))
        {
            edges.push_back(i + (T - a) / (b - a));
        }
    }
    if (edges.size() < 2)
        return {};
    // add in the new vector
    vector<double> runs;
    for (size_t j = 0; j + 1 < edges.size(); j++)
    {
        runs.push_back(edges[j + 1] - edges[j]);
    }
    return runs;
}
// converting 43 stripes to modules (between 1 and 4)
std::string Decoder::runsToModules(const std::vector<double> &runs) const
{
    if (runs.size() != stripes)
        return "";
    double total = 0;
    for (double r : runs)
        total += r;
    double module = total / modules;
    string bits;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < runs.size(); i++)
    {
        int n = (int)lround(runs[i] / module);
        if (n < 1 || n > 4)
            return "";
        bits += string(n, i % 2 == 0 ? '1' : '0');
    }
    return (bits.size() == modules) ? bits : "";
}
// matching digit with the given pattern
int Decoder::digitMatch(const std::string &seven, bool right)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++)
    {
        string pattern = patterns[i];
        if (right)
        {
            for (char &c : pattern)
                c = (c == '1') ? '0' : '1';
        }
        if (pattern == seven)
            return i;
    }
    return -1;
}
// converting modules to digits
std::string Decoder::modulesToDigits(const std::string &bits) const
{
    if (bits.size() != modules || bits.substr(0, 3) != "101" || bits.substr(64, 3) != "101" || bits.substr(31, 5) != "01010")
        return "";
    string digits;
    // left slot of barcode
    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++)
    {
        int d = digitMatch(bits.substr(3 + 7 * i, 7), false);
        if (d < 0)
            return "";
        digits += char('0' + d);
    }
    // right slot of barcode
    for (int j = 0; j < 4; j++)
    {
        int d = digitMatch(bits.substr(36 + 7 * j, 7), true);
        if (d < 0)
            return "";
        digits += char('0' + d);
    }
    return digits;
}
// is the check digit valid or not?
bool Decoder::isCheckDigit(const std::string &d) const
{ // 0482915 7
    int sum = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < 7; i++)
        sum += (d[i] - '0') * ((i % 2 == 0) ? 3 : 1);
    // eg 73 -> 80 80-73 =7
    return (10 - sum % 10) % 10 == d[7] - '0';
}
// store the six digits
bool Decoder::decodeSix(const std::string &bits, std::string &six) const
{
    six.clear();
    badCheckDigit = false;
    // 2 attempts
    for (int a = 0; a < 2; a++)
    { // straight and upside down case
        string b = (a == 0) ? bits : string(bits.rbegin(), bits.rend());
        string d = modulesToDigits(b);
        if (d.size() != 8)
            continue;
        if (!isCheckDigit(d))
        {
            badCheckDigit = true;
            continue;
        }
        // Product.txt stores the full eight-digit EAN-8, not the six-digit payload.
        six = d;
        badCheckDigit = false;
        return true;
    }
    return false;
}
// calls the above functions
bool Decoder::decodeCropping(const cv::Mat &gray, std::string &six) const
{
    badCheckDigit = false;
    six.clear();
    if (gray.empty() || gray.type() != CV_8UC1)
        return false;
    bool badCheck = false;
    // Thin bands tolerate tilted bars, avoids text/scratches crossing one row.
    for (int percent : {50, 40, 60, 30, 70, 20, 80})
    {
        const int y = gray.rows * percent / 100;
        const int top = std::max(0, y - 3);
        const int bottom = std::min(gray.rows, y + 4);
        cv::Mat band;
        cv::copyMakeBorder(gray.rowRange(top, bottom), band, 3, 3, 12, 12,
                           cv::BORDER_CONSTANT, cv::Scalar(255));
        const auto runs = scanLineRuns(band);
        // A barcode can be surrounded by other dark objects on the scan line.
        for (size_t start = 0; start + stripes <= runs.size(); start += 2)
        {
            const vector<double> candidate(runs.begin() + start, runs.begin() + start + stripes);
            const string bits = runsToModules(candidate);
            if (!bits.empty() && decodeSix(bits, six))
                return true;
            badCheck = badCheck || badCheckDigit;
        }
    }
    badCheckDigit = badCheck;
    return false;
}
// displays 'badCheck' for a bad check digit
// else shows error in terminal
bool Decoder::isBadCheck() const
{
    return badCheckDigit;
}

FILE: /README.md

<h1 align="center">🏷️ BarcodeHub</h1>

<p align="center">
  <b>A C++17-based Point of Sale system with barcode scanning, product management, order processing, and billing.</b>
  <br />
  Developed as an academic project for Introduction to Software Engineering at FAST-NUCES.
</p>

<p align="center">
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/C%2B%2B-17-00599C?style=for-the-badge&logo=cplusplus&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Qt-6-41CD52?style=for-the-badge&logo=qt&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/OpenCV-5C3EE8?style=for-the-badge&logo=opencv&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/CMake-Build%20System-064F8C?style=for-the-badge&logo=cmake&logoColor=white" />
  <img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Platform-Windows%20x64-0078D4?style=for-the-badge&logo=windows&logoColor=white" />
</p>

---

## 📌 Project Overview

**BarcodeHub** is a C++ desktop application being developed as a barcode-based Point of Sale (POS) system.

The project extends its initial barcode generation and reading capabilities into a GUI-based retail workflow, allowing cashiers to scan products, retrieve product information, manage orders, and generate bills. Later increments add category-based barcodes, inventory management, discounts, and management reports.

Developed incrementally, BarcodeHub emphasizes modularity, code quality, and reusability.

---

## ✨ Key Features

### v1.0.0

- 🏷️ Barcode generation for supported six-digit numeric product codes.
- 📖 Barcode reading functionality.
- 🖥️ Qt Widgets-based desktop application foundation.

### v2.0.0

- 📷 Camera-based barcode scanning.
- 📦 Product lookup and description from `Product.txt`.
- 🛒 Cart and order management.
- 📊 Product stock validation.
- 🧾 Automated bill generation.
- 💳 Cash, card, and wallet payment modes.
- 📁 File-based product, order, and billing records.
- 🎨 GUI-based Point of Sale workflow.

### v3.0.0

- 🗂️ Product category management, with the first two barcode digits representing the category.
- ➕ Manager tools to generate barcodes and to add or edit products.
- 📦 Inventory view and inventory updates, with stock updated on every order.
- ✏️ Opening and editing existing orders (paid orders only within 7 days of payment and after manager approval).
- ⚡ Fast order recording, with easy on-hold and resume.
- 🧾 Extended bills with bill details, amount received, and balance returned.
- 🌦️ Seasonal discounts from `Seasons.txt`, plus special discounts of up to 8 percent.
- 🖨️ Bill printing as PDF.
- 📈 Management reports: incomplete orders, completed orders, cash sales, all sales, top ten most sold items, and top ten items by remaining stock.
- 👥 Separate cashier and manager features.

---

## 🔄 POS Workflow

The POS workflow consists of four main modules:

1. **Product Management** — Retrieve product information and validate stock.
2. **Camera & Scanner** — Scan product barcodes using a connected camera.
3. **Order Management** — Record orders and manage product quantities.
4. **Billing** — Calculate totals, generate bills, and record payment information.

---

## 📄 Data Management

BarcodeHub uses text files to store its records:

| File | Purpose | Increment |
|---|---|---|
| `Product.txt` | Product specifications and available stock | 2 (category ID added in 3) |
| `Order.txt` | Customer and order information | 2 |
| `OrderDetail.txt` | Products and quantities associated with orders | 2 |
| `Bill.txt` | Generated bill information and payment status | 2 (extended in 3) |
| `PCategory.txt` | Product categories (ID, name, acronym) | 3 (planned) |
| `BillDetail.txt` | Product lines belonging to each bill | 3 (planned) |
| `Seasons.txt` | Seasonal discount periods and their status | 3 (planned) |

Each record follows the specified field order and uses `#` as its end-of-record marker.

FILE: /Run-BarcodeHub.cmd
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
@echo off
setlocal enabledelayedexpansion
title BarcodeHub POS and Billing System Launcher

:: Always run from the project root directory
cd /d "%~dp0"

echo ====================================================
echo  BarcodeHub POS and Billing System - Auto Build and Run
echo  Author: Ali Abdullah (25L-3022)
echo ====================================================
echo.

:: Detect toolchain paths
set "QT_ROOT=C:\Qt\6.11.0\mingw_64"
set "QT_TOOLS=C:\Qt\Tools"
set "MINGW_BIN="
set "CMAKE_BIN="
set "NINJA_BIN="

if exist "%QT_TOOLS%\mingw1310_64\bin\g++.exe" (
    set "MINGW_BIN=%QT_TOOLS%\mingw1310_64\bin"
) else if exist "C:\mingw64\bin\g++.exe" (
    set "MINGW_BIN=C:\mingw64\bin"
)

if exist "%QT_TOOLS%\CMake_64\bin\cmake.exe" (
    set "CMAKE_BIN=%QT_TOOLS%\CMake_64\bin"
) else (
    where cmake.exe >nul 2>&1 && set "CMAKE_BIN="
)

if exist "%QT_TOOLS%\Ninja\ninja.exe" (
    set "NINJA_BIN=%QT_TOOLS%\Ninja"
) else (
    where ninja.exe >nul 2>&1 && set "NINJA_BIN="
)

:: Ensure tools are in PATH
if defined MINGW_BIN set "PATH=!MINGW_BIN!;!PATH!"
if exist "%QT_ROOT%\bin" set "PATH=%QT_ROOT%\bin;!PATH!"
if defined CMAKE_BIN set "PATH=!CMAKE_BIN!;!PATH!"
if defined NINJA_BIN set "PATH=!NINJA_BIN!;!PATH!"

set "BUILD_DIR=build\qt-mingw"
set "EXE=%BUILD_DIR%\BarcodeHub.exe"

:: Step 1: Configure CMake if build directory does not exist
if not exist "%BUILD_DIR%\build.ninja" (
    echo [*] Initializing project configuration with Qt 6...
    cmake -S . -B "%BUILD_DIR%" -G Ninja -DCAMERA=OFF -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH="%QT_ROOT%"
    if errorlevel 1 (
        echo.
        echo [ERROR] CMake configuration failed. Please check paths.
        pause
        exit /b 1
    )
)

:: Step 2: Build project (fast incremental build - picks up any code changes automatically)
echo [*] Building BarcodeHub POS System...
cmake --build "%BUILD_DIR%" --parallel
if errorlevel 1 (
    echo.
    echo [ERROR] Build failed! Check compiler error messages above.
    pause
    exit /b 1
)

:: Step 3: Launch BarcodeHub POS application
echo [*] Launching BarcodeHub POS System...

:: Ensure desktop shortcut with custom icon exists on this PC
powershell -NoProfile -Command "$desktop = [Environment]::GetFolderPath('Desktop'); $lnk = Join-Path $desktop 'BarcodeHub POS System.lnk'; if (!(Test-Path $lnk)) { $ws = New-Object -ComObject WScript.Shell; $s = $ws.CreateShortcut($lnk); $s.TargetPath = '%~dp0Run-BarcodeHub.cmd'; $s.WorkingDirectory = '%~dp0'; $s.IconLocation = '%~dp0assets\pos_icon.ico,0'; $s.Description = 'BarcodeHub Point of Sale (POS) and Billing System'; $s.Save() }" >nul 2>&1

start "" "%EXE%"

:: Exit launcher window cleanly
exit /b 0

FILE: /Run-Camera-Evaluation.cmd
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0"
set "EVALUATION_DIR=%~dp0build\evaluation-camera"
if not exist "%EVALUATION_DIR%\BarcodeHub.exe" (
    echo Camera evaluation build is missing.
    echo Follow docs\evaluation\README.md to configure and build it first.
    pause
    exit /b 1
)
if not exist "%EVALUATION_DIR%\data\Product.txt" (
    echo Evaluation data is missing. Rebuild BarcodeHub to seed the data folder.
    pause
    exit /b 1
)
rem The separate build data folder preserves the repository's original records.
set "BARCODEHUB_DATA=%EVALUATION_DIR%\data"
start "" /D "%EVALUATION_DIR%" "%EVALUATION_DIR%\BarcodeHub.exe"
exit /b 0
